He grasped the sword and sat still, trembling all over; but when the smoke cleared away the whole aspect of the room was changed; the silken hangings, and gold, and pearls, and flowers, were all gone, and he was sitting in a grim gray chamber like a vault, and in front of him stood the yellow hag, whose eyes shone spitefully and her lips laughed wickedly; but in one hand she held what it made Michael rejoice to see. It was a soft pink feathery thing, with wings, but shaped like a heart, and it trembled and quivered in her hand.

===Chinese===
迈克尔一跃而起，把杯摔了出去。红宝石色的酒泼在地板上，接着是一声骇人的巨响，像炸雷一般，满屋腾起烟雾，只听得阵阵狂呼乱叫。

他握紧宝剑，坐着不动，浑身发抖。等烟散了，满屋的模样全变了：丝绸帷幔、金器、珍珠、花朵，统统不见了，他坐在一间阴森的灰色屋子里，活像一座地窖。他面前站着那黄衣老妖婆，眼里闪着毒光，嘴唇挂着奸笑；可她一只手里托着一样东西，叫迈克尔一见就欢喜。那是一件粉红色的、软茸茸的小东西，生着翅膀，形状却像一颗心，在她手里瑟瑟发抖。

===Original===
“Take it,” she cried, “for well have you won it. Take it, and tell the Queen how many years of toil and labour her proud words and boasting have cost. Then when you see her, from whom it was stolen, let it fly, but first say over it these words:—

> “Heart of Joan

 Lost and won

 Fly back home,

 Thy journey’s done.

 Take back joy

 Take back pain

 Heart of Joan,

 Fly home again.”

and it will fly to her side, and you will see it no more; and now begone.”

===Chinese===
“拿去吧，”她喊道，“这是你挣来的，受之无愧。拿去告诉王后，她那些骄傲的话、那些夸口，叫人赔上了多少年的辛劳苦楚。等你见到那个被偷走了心的人，就放它飞，只是先要对着它念下面这些话：——

> “琼的心，

失落又复还，

飞回家门，

旅途已完。

带回欢喜，

带回悲酸，

琼的心啊，

再回家园。”

它就会飞到她身边，你再也不会见到它了。走吧。”

===Original===
Michael seized the heart with a cry of joy and exultation, and then turned and fled from the room through an open iron door, and passed through the passages, no longer softly carpeted and hung with silk, but dreary and bare, made of cold stone, down which his footsteps echoed and clashed.

He hurried from the castle as quickly as might be, and once outside did not stop to look for the old man or the monster, but swam the moat, and went straight to where his boat lay moored as he had left it, nearly seven years before, and never paused till he had rowed so far that the gray castle and the shore had almost passed from view. At last he came again to the shore where he had bought his boat of the fishermen, and here he went on land, and started to walk till he had reached Joan’s country, and her father’s castle.

===Chinese===
迈克尔一把抓过那颗心，发出一声欢喜得意的欢呼，转身从一扇敞开的铁门逃出屋子，穿过一条条走廊。走廊里再没有柔软的地毯和丝绸帷幔，只有冷冰冰的石头，凄凉光秃，他的脚步声在下面回荡、铿然作响。

他尽快地逃出城堡，一出大门，也不停下寻找老人和那妖怪，泅过壕沟，径直奔向他泊船的地方——船还同近七年前他离开时一样泊着。他不停歇地划呀划，直划到那灰色的城堡和海岸差不多望不见了才歇。末了，他又回到当初向渔夫们买船的海岸，上了岸，便一路步行，直走到琼的国度、她父亲的城堡。

===Original===
He had no money, and his clothes were rags, his hair was thin and gray, and his shoulders bent. He looked like a poor beggar, and he had to beg food as he went, or he would have been starved. Still, he was ready to cry for joy, because he took with him the little soft heart he had gone so far to find.

He trudged on both day and night, making great haste, for he knew that the seven years were almost gone, and he was afraid lest already he might be too late, and find that Joan had married someone else. At last, after many weary miles, he reached her country, and drew near to the palace where she lived, and here he found that the people were all decorating their houses, and making preparations as if for some great festival.

===Chinese===
他身无分文，衣衫褴褛，头发稀疏花白，双肩佝偻，看上去就是个穷叫花子，一路上不得不讨饭吃，不然早饿死了。饶是这样，他还是欢喜得直想落泪——因为他随身带着那颗软软的小心脏，那可是他跑了那么远的路才寻到的。

他日夜赶路，急急如飞，因为他知道七年差不多满了，只怕已经迟了，琼已嫁了别人。走了许多许多疲惫的里路，他终于到了她的国度，走近她住的宫殿。只见家家户户都在装点门面，张罗忙碌，像要过什么盛大的节日。

===Original===
He stopped and begged for food from a woman who stood by a cottage door, and when she had given him some bread, as he ate it he asked her to tell him what went on in the country, and why there was such rejoicing.

“It is for the marriage of the King’s daughter Joan,” said the woman; “Tomorrow she is to be married to old King Lambert, and the wedding will be very grand, but none of the country folk like it, for he is old and ugly, and they say he does not love her at all, but only marries her that he may be king of this country as well as his own. The Queen is in sore distress about it, and for seven years refused her consent; but they will be over tomorrow, and so they will be wed, and the guests are already beginning to arrive at the palace, and each one brings some splendid gift.”

===Chinese===
他停下脚步，向一个站在农舍门边的女人讨口饭吃。女人给了他一些面包，他一边吃，一边请她讲讲国里出了什么事，为何这样欢腾。

“为的是国王的女儿琼要成亲呀，”女人说，“明天她就要嫁给老兰伯特国王（King Lambert）了，婚礼要办得盛大得很。可是庄户人家没一个乐意——那国王又老又丑，人们说他压根儿不爱她，娶她不过是图连这个国家也归他做国王。王后为这事愁得要命，七年了都不肯点头；可七年明天就满了，他们就要成亲了。宾客已经陆续进宫，个个都带着贵重的礼物。”

===Original===
“I will be a guest at that wedding,” cried Michael; “And I bring the best gift of all for the bride;” and he hurried on again, not heeding the woman’s scorn and laughter.

When he came to the palace, he found that it was hung with flags, and arches of flowers were erected in front of it, and grand lords and ladies, and servants stood at the door to receive the guests who came.

Michael went as near as he dared, afraid lest he should be driven away by the servants, and then he saw a little foot-page, and he went to him and said,

“Please tell me where is the Princess Joan, and what she is doing.”

===Chinese===
“那场婚礼我也要做个宾客，”迈克尔喊道，“我给新娘带来的，是所有礼物里最好的。”他又匆匆赶路去了，全不理会女人的讥笑。

他来到宫殿前，只见宫里挂满了旗子，门前搭起一座座鲜花拱门，尊贵的勋贵命妇和仆役们站在门口，迎接前来赴宴的宾客。

迈克尔壮着胆子尽量往前凑，生怕被仆役们赶开。他看见一个小听差，就走过去说：

“劳驾告诉我，琼公主在哪儿，她在做什么？”

===Original===
“She is sitting with the King and Queen and King Lambert in the stateroom, to receive the guests and accept the presents they bring,” said the page.

“I am a guest, and I bring a present for her,” cried Michael; “Tell me how I shall get into the palace that I may give it to her.”

On hearing this the page burst out laughing, and told the other servants what he said. And they were very angry, and seized Michael, and some would have ducked him in the pond, and some would have taken him before the King, but they said, “Not now—wait till the wedding is over tomorrow, and then we will see how he will punish the beggar-man for his impertinence.”

So they took him off to a stone tower outside the garden gates and thrust him into it, and locked the door, and there was only one little window high up and barred across with bars, and from it he could see the palace and the gardens.

===Chinese===
“她正同国王、王后和兰伯特国王坐在正殿里，接待宾客，收受礼物呢。”小听差说。

“我就是个宾客，我给她带来了一件礼物，”迈克尔喊道，“告诉我怎样才能进宫，好把礼物交给她。”

小听差听了放声大笑，把他这话学给了其他仆役听。众人勃然大怒，一把揪住迈克尔，有的要把他按到池塘里浸一浸，有的要把他拖去见国王；可他们商量说：“现在不行——等到明天婚礼完了，再看国王怎么发落这叫花子的无礼。”

于是他们把他拖到园门外一座石头塔里，将他推进去，锁上门。塔上只有一扇高高的小窗，窗上钉着栏条；从那里，他望得见宫殿和花园。

===Original===
Then at last he gave way to despair. “Of what avail were all my years of toil, and for what am I gray and old before my time,” he cried, “if after all, when I have earned that for which I worked so long, I may not give it to Joan, but must remain a prisoner and see her pass by to marry someone else?” and he threw himself on the ground and cried aloud.

===Chinese===
他终于绝望了。“我这许多年的苦熬，算得了什么？我又为的是什么，未老先衰、头发花白？”他喊道，“到头来，我苦干这么久挣来的东西，竟不能交到琼手里，只配做个囚徒，眼睁睁看她走过去嫁别人？”他扑倒在地上，放声大哭。

===Original===
At night as he lay and mourned, he heard sounds of merrymaking, and music and laughter from the castle. Sometimes he called out, “Joan! Joan! I am here—I who have worked for you for years, and brought home your stolen heart, and now will you wed King Lambert in spite of all?” sometimes he beat against the bars of the prison window, but all in vain, and at last, when all sound had ceased from the castle, he lay silent upon the ground, caring no more for life.

===Chinese===
夜里，他躺着悲叹，听见城堡里欢宴的声浪、音乐和笑声。他有时喊：“琼！琼！我在这儿——我为你苦了这么多年，把你被偷的心带回来了，如今你还是要不顾一切，嫁给兰伯特国王吗？”有时他捶打囚窗的栏条，可全是白费。末了，城堡那边一切声响都歇了，他静静地躺在地上，对性命也不再在意了。

===Original===
When the sun rose, and there was again a stir without, he got up and looked from the window, and saw the old nurse who walked by herself in the garden, and she looked very sad. Then Michael called out, “Do you not know me? You at least, who bid me go, and praised me then, should remember me now.” On hearing this the old nurse drew near the prison window, and looked at him, and said, “Who are you, and why are you here? My eyes are old, and my ears are deaf, but I think I have seen you, and heard your voice before.”

“Seven years ago,” said Michael, “I too was a bridegroom, who came to wed your Princess, and for seven long years have I worked, that I might bring home to her the heart she had not. Go and ask your Queen, why she has broken her pledge to wait for seven years, till Prince Michael should return.”

“Prince Michael! Is it really Prince Michael?” cried the old nurse joyfully. “And you come in time, for our Princess is not married yet, and she must pass by here, on her way to church. So you shall call to her as she passes by, and speak for yourself.”

“Then keep near and tell me when she comes,” said Michael, “lest she go by without seeing me.”

===Chinese===
太阳升起，外面又骚动起来。他起身朝窗外望去，看见老乳母独自在花园里散步，一脸愁容。迈克尔喊道：“您不认得我了吗？当年是您叫我去，还夸了我——至少您，总该记得我啊。”老乳母听了，走近囚窗，端详着他，说：“你是谁？怎么在这儿？我眼睛老了，耳朵也聋了，可我好像见过你，听过你的声音。”

“七年前，”迈克尔说，“我也是个新郎，来娶你们的公主；这漫漫七年，我苦干至今，就为把她缺的那颗心带回来给她。去问你们的王后：她为什么食言，不肯等满七年，等迈克尔王子回来。”

“迈克尔王子！真是迈克尔王子吗？”老乳母欢喜地喊道，“你来得正好，我们的公主还没成亲呢。她去教堂，必定打这儿经过，等她路过时，你就喊她，自己同她说。”

“那您就守在近旁，她来的时候告诉我一声，”迈克尔说，“免得她走过去，看不见我。”

===Original===
Presently the whole castle was astir, and trumpets were sounding, and clarions ringing. Then when the sun was high, Michael heard the tramping of horses, and the sound of music, and the old nurse said to him, “Here she is,” and he looked between the bars of the prison window and saw a grand procession, and his heart gave a bound, for in their midst, in a golden gown, and seated on a white palfrey, was Princess Joan, and she looked just as lovely as when he went away seven years before.

On one side of her rode her father and mother, and the Queen’s face was most mournful, and her eyes were red with crying. On the other rode an ugly old man, whom Michael guessed to be King Lambert, and he smiled and bowed to the people, but they muttered and grumbled, when they looked at him, and saw how ugly and wicked, he looked.

===Chinese===
不多时，整座城堡都沸腾起来，号角吹响，铜号齐鸣。日头升高时，迈克尔听见马蹄踏踏、乐声悠扬。老乳母对他说：“她来了。”他从囚窗的栏条间望出去，只见一支盛大的仪仗。他的心猛地一跳——队伍正中，琼公主穿着一身金色的衣裙，骑在一匹白色的小马上，风姿同七年前他离去时一样可爱。

她一侧骑马走着她父亲和母亲；王后满面悲容，眼睛哭得通红。另一侧是一个丑陋的老头儿，迈克尔猜他就是兰伯特国王。他朝百姓们微笑鞠躬，百姓们望着他，见他那副又丑又恶的相貌，却只是嘟囔抱怨。

===Original===
When Michael saw them coming, he took from his bosom the little pink heart, and stroked it fondly as he whispered over it,

> “Heart of Joan

 Lost and won

 Fly back home,

 Thy journey’s done.

 Take back joy

 Take back pain

 Heart of Joan,

 Fly home again;”

===Chinese===
迈克尔见他们来了，便从怀里取出那颗粉红的小心脏，疼爱地抚着它，对它低声念道：

> “琼的心，

失落又复还，

飞回家门，

旅途已完。

带回欢喜，

带回悲酸，

琼的心啊，

再回家园；”

===Original===
and at once it spread its wings and fluttered through the bars of the prison, and over the heads of the people, who shouted, “Look at the pink bird!” For a moment it rested at the side of the Princess Joan, and then disappeared. She gave a scream, and cried,

“My mother! My father! What has happened? Oh see, it is Michael who has returned!” and ere they could stop her she had turned her palfrey’s head towards the prison window, and pushed her white arms through the bars to clasp the Prince.

===Chinese===
话音才落，它便张开翅膀，扑棱棱穿过囚窗的栏条，飞过众人的头顶。人们喊起来：“瞧那粉色的小鸟！”它在琼公主身侧停了一瞬，随即不见了。公主尖叫一声，喊道：

“母亲！父亲！这是怎么了？哦，瞧，是迈克尔回来了！”众人还没来得及拦住她，她已拨转马头，直奔囚窗而来，把雪白的手臂从栏条间伸进去，要搂住王子。

===Original===
“Michael, my love!” she cried, “How gray and worn you are now. How hard you must have laboured for me through these long years. Now, how shall I pay you, save by loving you all my life!” and she tried to beat down the bars of the prison window.

===Chinese===
“迈克尔，我的爱人！”她喊道，“你如今这样灰白、这样憔悴！这些年，你为我受了多少苦啊！如今叫我怎么报答你——只有一辈子爱你了！”她使劲要扳倒囚窗的栏条。

===Original===
When the people heard her, they cried, “It is Prince Michael, who went seven years ago, and who we all thought was dead, and he is returned in time to marry our Princess. Now will we indeed have a wedding, and she shall marry the Prince who has toiled so long for her;” and King and Queen and people laughed for joy. ’Twas in vain for King Lambert to rage, and cry that the Princess was betrothed to him.

“Nay!” said the Queen, “She has been pledged to Prince Michael for seven years. We are grieved for your sake, King Lambert, but we cannot break our royal word.”

===Chinese===
百姓们听了她的话，喊道：“是迈克尔王子！七年前走的那个，我们都当他死了，他回来得正好，正好娶我们的公主！这下可真有婚礼办了——她就嫁给这位为她苦了这么久的王子！”国王、王后和百姓都欢喜得笑了。兰伯特国王再怎么暴跳如雷，喊叫公主已许配给他，也是枉然。

“不！”王后说，“七年来，她一直许给迈克尔王子。兰伯特国王，我们为您难过，可金口玉言，不能反悔。”

===Original===
Then the people burst into the prison and brought out Michael, all torn and gray as he was, and Princess Joan kissed him before them all, and begged that he would marry her at once, that everyone might see how well she loved him and how grateful she was. So they brought a fine white horse with a grand gold saddle, and jewelled bridle, and placed Michael upon it, and he rode to church beside the Princess, and married her, and the people threw flowers before them, and bells rang and trumpets sounded, and all were glad.

And when it was done Michael was dressed in purple and gold, and messengers were sent to his father and mother and the old wizard, that they might come and see how he had come home victorious, and rejoicings filled the whole country.

“For now we are sure of a good King,” the people said. “See, he has already shown what he can do. Surely no one else could ever have found the heart of Princess Joan.”

===Chinese===
于是百姓们冲进囚塔，把迈克尔带了出来——尽管他衣衫破碎、须发灰白。琼公主当着众人吻了他，恳求他立刻同她成婚，好叫人人都看见她多么爱他、多么感激他。众人牵来一匹神骏的白马，配上华丽的金鞍和缀珠的辔头，扶迈克尔上马。他傍着公主骑马到教堂，同她成了婚。百姓们在他们面前抛洒鲜花，钟声齐鸣，号角喧天，人人欢喜。

礼成之后，迈克尔换上了紫袍金衣；信使们被打发去见他的父母和老巫师，请他们来看他如何凯旋归来。欢庆弥漫了整个国家。

“这下我们保准有位好国王了，”百姓们说，“瞧，他已经露了本事。除了他，谁还能找得回琼公主的心呢？”

## The Pedlar’s Pack / 小贩的货担

===Original===
A pedlar was toiling along a dusty road carrying his pack on his back, when he saw a donkey grazing by the wayside.

“Good day, friend,” said he. “If you have nothing to do, perhaps you would not mind carrying my load for me for a little.”

===Chinese===
一个小贩背着货担，正在尘土飞扬的大路上吃力地赶路，忽见一头驴在路旁啃草。

“您好啊，朋友，”他说，“你若手头无事，可愿替我把这担子驮上一小程？”

===Original===
“If I do so, what will you give me?” said the donkey.

“I will give you two pieces of gold,” said the pedlar, but he did not speak the truth, for he knew he had no gold to give.

===Chinese===
“我若驮了，你给我什么好处？”驴说。

“我给你两枚金币，”小贩说。可他说的是假话，因为他明知自己压根儿没有金币可给。

===Original===
“Agreed,” said the donkey. So they journeyed on together in a very friendly manner, the donkey carrying the pedlar’s pack, and the pedlar walking by his side. After a time they met a raven, who was looking for worms in the roadside, and the donkey called out to him,

“Good-morrow, black friend. If you are going our way, you would do well to sit upon my back and drive away the flies, which worry me sadly.”

===Chinese===
“一言为定，”驴说。于是两个便十分亲热地结伴赶路：驴驮着小贩的货担，小贩走在它身旁。走了一程，他们遇见一只渡鸦，正在路边觅虫，驴便朝它喊道：

“早安，黑衣朋友。你若与我们同路，不如坐上我的背来，替我赶赶苍蝇——它们烦得我好苦。”

===Original===
“And what will you pay me to do this?” asked the raven.

“Money is no object to me,” said the donkey, “so I will give you three pieces of gold.” And he too knew he was making a false promise, for he had no gold at all to give.

===Chinese===
“那么我干这差事，你出多少工钱？”渡鸦问。

“钱嘛，在我算不得什么，”驴说，“我给你三枚金币。”它也一样，明知自己许的是空愿，因为它压根儿一个金币也没有。

===Original===
“Agreed,” said the raven. So they went on in high good humour, the donkey carrying the pedlar’s wares, and the raven sitting on the donkey’s back driving away the flies.

After a time they met a hedge-sparrow, and the raven called out to it,

===Chinese===
“一言为定，”渡鸦说。于是他们兴高采烈地继续赶路：驴驮着小贩的货物，渡鸦坐在驴背上赶着苍蝇。

又走了一程，他们遇见一只篱雀，渡鸦便朝它喊道：

===Original===
“Good day, little cousin. Do you want to earn a little money? If so, bring me some worms from the bank as we go along, for I had no breakfast, and am very hungry.”

“What will you give me for it?” asked the hedge-sparrow.

===Chinese===
“你好啊，小表弟。你想挣几个小钱吗？想的话，趁我们一路走着，替我从土坡上衔几条虫子来吧——我没吃早饭，饿得很呢。”

“那你给我什么报酬？”篱雀问。

===Original===
“Let us say four pieces of gold,” said the raven grandly; “for I have saved more during my long life than I know how to spend.” But he knew this was not true, for he had not saved any gold at all.

“Very well,” said the hedge-sparrow, and so on they went, the donkey carrying the pedlar’s pack, and the raven keeping the flies away from the donkey, and the hedge-sparrow bringing worms to the raven.

===Chinese===
“就算四枚金币吧，”渡鸦大模大样地说，“我活了这一大把年纪，攒下的钱多得都不知道怎么花呢。”可它心知这话不实，因为它压根儿一枚金币也不曾攒下。

“那好，”篱雀说。于是他们又往前走：驴驮着小贩的货担，渡鸦替驴赶苍蝇，篱雀给渡鸦衔虫子。

===Original===
Presently they saw in the distance a good-sized town, and the pedlar took out from his pack, some shawls and stuffs and hung them over the donkey’s back that the passersby might see, and buy if they were so disposed. On the top of the other goods lay a small scarlet blanket, and when he saw it the hedge-sparrow said to the pedlar,

“What will you take for that little blanket? It seems to be a good one. Name your price and you shall have it whatever it is, for I am badly in want of a blanket just now;” but as the hedge-sparrow had not a penny in the world, he knew he could not pay for it.

===Chinese===
不多时，他们望见远处有座像模像样的市镇。小贩从货担里取出几条披肩、几匹布料，搭在驴背上，好教过路人瞧见，动了心的便会来买。在旁的货物顶上，铺着一条猩红色的小毯子。篱雀一眼瞧见，便对小贩说：

“那条小毯子你卖多少钱？瞧着倒像条好毯子。你开个价吧，多少我都照付——我眼下正缺一条毯子呢。”可篱雀普天之下连一个铜子儿也没有，它知道自己根本付不出这笔钱。

===Original===
“The price of the blanket is five pieces of gold,” said the pedlar.

“That seems to me to be very dear,” said the hedge-sparrow. “I don’t mind giving you four pieces of gold for it, but five is too much.”

===Chinese===
“这毯子的价钱嘛，是五枚金币，”小贩说。

“依我看，这价钱太贵了，”篱雀说，“四枚金币我倒舍得给，五枚可就太多了。”

===Original===
“Agreed,” said the pedlar, and he chuckled to himself and thought, “Now I shall be able to pay the donkey, otherwise I might have had some trouble in getting rid of him.”

The hedge-sparrow flew to the raven’s side and whispered in his ear, “Please to pay me the four pieces of gold you owe me, for we are coming to a town, and I must be turning back.”

===Chinese===
“一言为定，”小贩说。他心里暗自好笑，想道：“这下子我可有钱付驴了，不然，想甩脱它只怕还有些麻烦呢。”

篱雀飞到渡鸦身旁，凑着它耳朵低声说：“请你把欠我的四枚金币付了吧——前面就要到市镇了，我得回头了。”

===Original===
“Four pieces of gold is really too much for bringing a few worms,” said the raven. “It is absurd to expect such payment, but I will give you three, and you shall have them almost immediately,” and he bent down over the donkey’s ear and whispered,

“My friend, it is time you paid me the three pieces of gold which you promised, for the pedlar will stop at this town, and you will not have to go farther with him.”

===Chinese===
“衔几条虫子就要四枚金币，实在太多了，”渡鸦说，“指望这样的报酬，未免太荒唐。不过我给你三枚，而且立马就给。”说着，它俯下身去，对着驴的耳朵悄声道：

“我的朋友，你许我的那三枚金币，该付给我啦——小贩到了这镇上就要歇脚，你也不必再陪他往前走了。”

===Original===
“On thinking it over,” said the donkey, “I have come to the conclusion that three pieces of gold are really a great deal too much to give for having a few flies driven away. You must have known that I was only joking when I said it, but I will let you have two, though I consider that it is much more than the job was worth;” and the donkey turned again to the pedlar, saying, “Now, good sir, your two pieces of gold, if you please.”

===Chinese===
“我细细掂量过了，”驴说，“得出的结论是：为赶几只苍蝇便付三枚金币，实在太多太多。我说那话时你该如何道，我不过是闹着玩罢了。不过两枚我可以给你——虽说依我看，这活计远远值不上这么多。”说罢，驴又转向小贩，道：“好啦，好心的先生，请把你那两枚金币给我吧。”

===Original===
“In a moment,” said the pedlar, and turning to the hedge-sparrow, said, “I really must have the money for the blanket at once.”

“So you shall,” answered the hedge-sparrow, and cried angrily to the raven, “I want my money now, and cannot wait.”

===Chinese===
“这就给，”小贩说着，又转向篱雀道：“毯子钱我可是要立时收清的。”

“这就付，”篱雀应道，又气呼呼地朝渡鸦喊：“我这就要我的钱，等不得啦。”

===Original===
“In an instant,” answered the raven, and again whispered to the donkey, “Why can’t you pay me honestly? I should be ashamed of trying to slip out of my debts in such a way.”

“I won’t keep you waiting a second,” said the donkey, and he turned once more to the pedlar and cried, “Come, give me my money. For shame! a man like you trying to cheat a poor beast like me.”

===Chinese===
“马上就给，”渡鸦答道，又对驴悄声道：“你怎么就不能爽爽快快地还钱？像这样想赖账，若换了我，都要臊得慌。”

“一秒钟也不叫你等，”驴说，随即又转向小贩喊道：“喂，把我的钱拿来！不害臊吗！你这么个人，竟想骗我这可怜的畜生。”

===Original===
Then the pedlar said to the hedge-sparrow, “Pay me for my blanket, or I’ll wring your neck.”

And the hedge-sparrow cried to the raven, “Give me my money or I’ll peck out your eyes.”

And the raven croaked to the donkey, “If you don’t pay me, I’ll bite off your tail.”

And the donkey again cried to the pedlar, “You dishonest wretch, pay me my money or I’ll kick you soundly.”

===Chinese===
于是小贩对篱雀说：“快付我毯子钱，不然我拧断你的脖子。”

篱雀朝渡鸦喊：“快给我钱，不然我啄出你的眼珠。”

渡鸦朝驴哑声叫道：“你不付钱，我就咬掉你的尾巴。”

驴又朝小贩喊：“你这奸诈的恶棍，快付我的钱，不然我狠狠踢你。”

===Original===
And they made such an uproar outside the walls of the town, that the beadle came out to see what it was all about. Each turned to him and began to complain of the other loudly.

“You are a set of rogues and vagabonds,” said the beadle, “and you shall all come before the mayor, and he’ll settle your quarrels pretty quickly, and treat you as you deserve.”

===Chinese===
他们在镇墙外闹得不可开交，镇上的差役（beadle）出来看是怎么回事。四个都抢着朝他大声数落起对方来。

“你们是一伙流氓加游民，”差役说，“都得去见镇长。他三下两下就能给你们断了这场官司，叫你们各得其所。”

===Original===
At this they all begged to be allowed to go away, each one saying he did not care about being paid at all. But the beadle would not listen to them, and led them straight away to the marketplace, where the mayor sat judging the people.

“Now, whom have we here?” cried he. “A pedlar, a donkey, a raven, and a hedge-sparrow. A set of worthless vagabonds, I’ll be bound! Let us hear what they have to say for themselves.”

===Chinese===
一听这话，他们个个都哀求放他们走路，都说自己压根儿不在乎拿不拿钱。可差役哪里肯听，径直把他们押到集市上——镇长正在那里坐堂问案呢。

“嗬，来的都是些什么人呀？”他喊道，“一个小贩、一头驴、一只渡鸦、一只篱雀。我敢保，准是一伙没出息的游民！来，且听听他们各自怎么说。”

===Original===
On this the pedlar began to complain of the hedge-sparrow, and the hedge-sparrow of the raven, and the raven of the donkey, and the donkey of the pedlar.

The mayor did not heed them much, but he eyed the pedlar’s pack, and at length interrupted them, and said,

===Chinese===
于是小贩数落篱雀，篱雀数落渡鸦，渡鸦数落驴，驴数落小贩。

镇长没怎么理会他们的吵嚷，只把眼睛盯着小贩的货担，末了打断他们，说道：

===Original===
“I am convinced that you are a set of good-for-nothing fellows, and one is quite as bad as the other, so I order that the pedlar be locked up in the prison, that the donkey be soundly well thrashed, and that the raven and the hedge-sparrow both have their tail-feathers pulled out, and then be turned out of the town. As for the blanket, it seems to me to be the only good thing in the whole matter, and as I cannot allow you to keep the cause of such a disturbance, I will take it for myself. Beadle, lead the prisoners away.”

===Chinese===
“我算看明白了，你们是一伙百无一用的家伙，一个跟一个一样坏。因此我判：小贩关进监狱；驴给我结结实实打一顿；渡鸦和篱雀都拔掉尾巴上的羽毛，然后赶出城去。至于那条毯子嘛——依我看，它是这整桩事情里唯一的好东西；这场祸事的根苗，我不能留给你们，我就自己收下了。差役，把犯人带下去。”

===Original===
So the beadle did as he was told, and the pedlar was locked up for many days in the prison.

“It is very sad to think to what straits an honest man may be brought,” he sighed to himself as he sat lamenting his hard fate. “In future this will be a warning to me to keep clear of hedge-sparrows. If the hedge-sparrow had paid me as he ought, I should not be here now.”

===Chinese===
差役奉命而行，小贩便在监狱里一关许多天。

“想想看，一个老实人竟会给逼到这般田地，真是可叹哪，”他坐在那儿哀叹自己命苦，自言自语道，“往后这可是个教训，教我远远躲开篱雀。当初篱雀若是照该当的付了钱，我如今也不至于蹲在这儿。”

===Original===
Meantime the donkey was being soundly well thrashed, and after each blow he cried,

“Alas! alas! See what comes to an innocent quadruped for having to do with human beings. Had the pedlar given me the money he owed, I should not now be beaten thus. In future I will never make a bargain with men.”

===Chinese===
与此同时，驴正挨着结结实实的鞭打，每挨一鞭，它便喊道：

“哎呀！哎呀！瞧瞧一个无辜的四脚牲口，只因为同人类打了交道，落得个什么下场。当初小贩若把欠我的钱给了我，我如今也不至于挨这般毒打。往后我再也不跟人类做买卖了。”

===Original===
The raven and the hedge-sparrow hopped out of the town by different roads, and both were very sad, for they had lost all their tail feathers, which the beadle had pulled out.

“Alas!” croaked the raven, “my fate is indeed a hard one. But it serves me right for trusting a donkey who goes on his feet and cannot fly. It is truly a warning to me never again to trust anything without a beak.”

===Chinese===
渡鸦和篱雀各走各路，一蹦一跳地出了镇子。两个都伤心得很，因为差役把它们的尾巴羽毛拔了个精光。

“哎呀！”渡鸦呱声叹道，“我的命可真是苦啊。可这也怨我自己，谁叫我信了一头只会迈开脚走路、不会飞的驴呢。这真是个教训：往后凡是没长喙的，我一概不信。”

===Original===
The hedge-sparrow was quite crestfallen, and could scarcely keep from tears. “It all comes of my being so taken in by that raven,” he sighed. “But I should have known that these large birds are never honest. In future I will be wise, and never make a bargain with anything bigger or stronger than myself.”

===Chinese===
篱雀垂头丧气，几乎忍不住要掉泪。“都怪我太信那只渡鸦，才落到这步田地，”它叹道，“可我早该如何道，这些大个儿的鸟就没有老实的。往后我要学乖了，凡是比我大、比我强的，一概不同它们做买卖。”

## The Bread of Discontent / 不满面包

===Original===
Once there was a baker who had a very bad, violent temper, and whenever a batch of bread was spoiled he flew into such a rage, that his wife and daughters dared not go near him. One day it happened that all his bread was burnt, and on this he stamped and raved with anger. He threw the loaves all about the floor, when one, burnt blacker than the rest, broke in half, and out of it crept a tiny thin black man, no thicker than an eel, with long arms and legs.

===Chinese===
从前有个面包师，脾气坏得出奇，火暴得很。每逢一炉面包烤坏了，他就大发雷霆，吓得老婆和女儿们都不敢近他的身。有一天，他的面包偏偏全烤糊了，他气得又跺脚又吼叫，把一个个面包满地乱扔。扔着扔着，其中一只糊得比旁的都黑的面包裂成两半，从里头爬出一个瘦精精的小黑妖（imp），身子不比一条鳝鱼粗，胳膊和腿倒是老长。

===Original===
“What are you making all this fuss about, Master Baker?” said he. “If you will give me a home in your oven I will see to the baking of your bread, and will answer for it that you shall never have so much as a loaf spoiled.”

===Chinese===
“面包师老板，你这么大吵大闹，闹的是哪一出啊？”他说，“你若肯让我在你家炉子里安个家，烤面包的事就交给我照管，我给你打包票：往后你连一只面包也不会烤坏。”

===Original===
“And pray what sort of bread would it be, if you were in the oven, and helped to bake it?” said the baker; “I think my customers might not like to eat it.”

===Chinese===
“那我倒要请问：你蹲在炉子里搭手烤出来的，会是种什么面包？”面包师说，“我看我的主顾们未必乐意吃。”

===Original===
“On the contrary,” said the imp, “they would like it exceedingly. It is true that it would make them rather unhappy, but that will not hurt you, as you need not eat it yourself.”

===Chinese===
“恰恰相反，”小黑妖说，“他们会喜欢得不得了呢。不错，这面包会叫他们相当不开心，可那伤不着你——横竖你自己又不必吃它。”

===Original===
“Why should it make them unhappy?” said the baker. “If it is good bread it won’t do anyone harm, and if it is bad they won’t buy it.”

===Chinese===
“为什么会叫他们不开心？”面包师说，“面包要是好，就吃不坏人；面包要是坏，他们压根儿就不会买。”

===Original===
“It will taste very good,” replied the imp, “but it will make all who eat it discontented, and they will think themselves very unfortunate whether they are so or no; but this will not do you any harm, and I promise you that you shall sell as much as you wish.”

===Chinese===
“味道好得很，”小黑妖答道，“只是谁吃了它，谁就不知足——不管自己是不是真倒霉，都只道自己是天底下顶苦命的人。可这半点儿也伤不着你，我担保你想卖多少，就能卖掉多少。”

===Original===
“Agreed!” said the baker. So the little imp crept into the oven and curled himself into the darkness behind, and the baker saw no more of him.

===Chinese===
“一言为定！”面包师说。于是小黑妖钻进炉子，蜷起身子，缩进后头的黑影里，从此面包师再没瞧见他。

===Original===
But next day he made a great batch of bread, and though he took no heed of the time when he put it in, and drew it out, just as he wanted it, it was done quite right—neither too dark nor too light—and the baker was in high good humour.

===Chinese===
可是第二天，他烤了满满一大炉面包；什么时候放进去、什么时候取出来，他全不曾留心，全凭自己高兴，出炉的火候却恰恰好——颜色不深也不浅——面包师乐得心花怒放。

===Original===
The first person who tasted the bread was the chief justice. He came down to breakfast in high spirits, for he had just heard that an old aunt was dead, and had left him a great deal of money. So he kissed his wife and chucked his daughters under the chin, and told them that he had good news for them. His old aunt had left him twenty thousand pounds in her will. On this his wife clapped her hands for joy, and his daughters ran to him and kissed him, and begged him to let them have some of it. So they all sat down to breakfast in great glee, but no sooner had the justice tasted the bread than his face fell.

===Chinese===
第一个尝这面包的，是大法官（the chief justice）。他兴冲冲地下楼来吃早饭，因为刚听说一位老姑妈过世了，留给他好大一笔钱。于是他吻了吻妻子，又抬手逗了逗女儿们的下巴颏儿，说有好消息告诉她们：老姑妈在遗嘱里留给他两万英镑。妻子一听，喜得直拍手；女儿们跑上来亲他，求他分一点儿给她们。一家人欢欢喜喜坐下吃早饭，谁知大法官一尝那面包，脸就沉了下来。

===Original===
“This is excellent bread,” he said, taking a large slice; “I wish everything else were as good;” and he heaved a deep sigh.

“Why?” cried his wife, who had not yet begun to eat. “This morning, I am sure, there is nothing for you to complain of.”

===Chinese===
“这面包真是没得挑，”他说着，切了厚厚一大片，“但愿样样东西都这样好才成。”说完深深叹了口气。

“叹什么气呀？”还没动早饭的妻子嚷道，“今儿早上，我敢保你没有一桩事可抱怨的。”

===Original===
“Nay!” said the mayor; “it is very nice to have twenty thousand pounds, but think how much nicer it would have been if it had been thirty. How much more one could have done with that! Or even if it had been twenty-five thousand pounds, or even twenty-one. Twenty-one thousand pounds is a very nice sum of money, but twenty thousand pounds is no good at all. I am not sure that it would not be better not to have had any.”

“Nonsense!” cried his wife, who was now eating her breakfast also; “you are very wicked to be so discontented; but one thing I do say. It would have been much nicer if we had had it when we were young and better able to enjoy it. Money is very little use to people at our time of life. It would have been really nice if we had had it fifteen years ago. As it is, I can’t say I care much for it, and it makes me sad to think we did not get it before.”

===Chinese===
“哪儿的话！”大法官说，“有两万英镑固然好，可你想想，若是三万，那该多好！那能多办多少事！再不济，两万五也好哇，哪怕两万一呢。两万一千英镑是笔顶体面的家当，两万英镑可就顶不了什么用了。我简直拿不准——兴许压根儿一个子儿不落，反倒干净些。”

“胡说！”这会儿也吃起早饭来的妻子嚷道，“你这人真真坏心眼，这样不知足。可有一句话我得说：这钱若是趁咱们年轻、还消受得起的时候到手，那才叫好呢。钱到了咱们这把年纪，没什么大用处。十五年前得了它，才算真的美。如今嘛，老实说，我不怎么稀罕它；一想到没早点儿到手，我心里就发愁。”

===Original===
“Nay,” cried the daughters; “in that case how much better it would have been for us to have it instead of you; we are young, and able to enjoy ourselves, and we could have given you a little of it if you’d liked, but we could have been very happy with the rest; as it is, it is no pleasure to us.”

So they fell to quarrelling about the money, and by the time breakfast was done, they all had tears in their eyes, and felt discontented and unhappy.

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛，”女儿们嚷道，“那样的话，这钱给我们才好，别给你们！我们年轻，享得了这个福。你们若愿意，我们匀你们一点儿，余下的尽够我们快快活活过日子——如今这样，对我们可是一点儿乐处也没有。”

于是这一家子为钱吵作一团。一顿早饭吃完，个个眼里噙着泪花，个个满心委屈，快活不起来了。

===Original===
The next person to eat the bread was the village doctor. All night long he had been sitting up with a man who had broken his leg, and he had feared lest he should die, but as morning came he saw he would live, so he returned home to his wife in very good spirits, although he was sadly tired. The wife had already had her breakfast, but she had made all ready for her husband, with a loaf of the baker’s new bread.

===Chinese===
第二个吃这面包的，是村里的医生。他守了一整夜，陪着一个摔断了腿的人，一直怕他死了；到天亮时，眼见人能活下来了，他才回家见妻子，人虽然累得慌，兴致却高。妻子自己已经吃过早饭，把丈夫的那份预备得妥妥帖帖，里头有一只面包师家新出炉的面包。

===Original===
“See, dear husband,” she said, “here is your breakfast, and some nice bread quite new, because I know you like it. How glad we ought to be, that this poor man is likely to live.”

“Yes, indeed,” said the doctor; “being up all night is tiring work, but I don’t grudge it when I know that it does some good,” and then he began to eat. “I am not sure, after all, that I have done such a good thing in curing this man. It is true that his broken leg hurt him very much, but perhaps when he is well again, he may break his back, and that would be much worse. Perhaps I had better have left him to die. I daresay when he is quite well, all kinds of misfortunes will befall him; I had much better have let him alone.”

===Chinese===
“瞧，亲爱的，”她说，“这是你的早饭，还有刚出炉的喷香面包——我知道你爱吃。这可怜人有指望活下来了，咱们该多欢喜啊。”

“可不是，”医生说，“熬一整夜是累人的活计，可只要知道不白熬，我就不抱怨。”说着他吃了起来。“话说回来，治好这个人，究竟算不算一桩好事，我倒拿不准了。腿断了自然疼得厉害，可等他养好了，说不定摔断脊梁骨，那可就更糟了。当初也许还不如随他死了干净。等他大好之后，保不齐各样灾祸都要找上他；看来我还是别管他的好。”

===Original===
“Why,” cried his wife in surprise, “what are you saying, husband? Are you not a doctor, and is it not your business to cure people? And when you succeed ought you not to be glad?”

“I wish I were not a doctor,” said the husband, sighing. “It would be much better if there were no doctors at all;” and he sat and lamented, and nothing his wife could say, could cheer him.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，”妻子吃了一惊，叫道，“你说什么呢，当家的？你不正是医生吗？给人治病不正是你的本分吗？治好了病，你不正该欢喜吗？”

“我倒巴不得自己不是个医生，”丈夫叹着气说，“这世上压根儿就没有医生，才叫好呢。”他坐在那儿长吁短叹，妻子说什么，也宽慰不了他半分。

===Original===
In a pretty little cottage near the doctor’s house lived a young couple, who were newly married, and were as happy as the day was long. Their cottage was covered with roses, and filled with pretty things, and they had everything their hearts could desire. This morning they both came down smiling and happy, and the young wife kissed her husband, and sang for joy. So they sat down to breakfast, chattering like two birds in a nest; but no sooner had the husband tasted the bread than his face fell, and he was silent for a time; then he said,

===Chinese===
离医生家不远，有一座漂亮的小村舍，住着一对新婚的小两口，日子过得说不出的快活。屋外爬满蔷薇，屋里摆满漂亮玩意儿，心想要什么就有什么。这天早上，两口儿笑吟吟地下楼来，年轻的妻子吻了吻丈夫，欢喜得唱起歌来。两人坐下吃早饭，像窝里两只小鸟似的叽叽喳喳说个不停；可丈夫一尝那面包，脸色便沉了下来，好一阵子不作声，然后说道：

===Original===
“It is a very terrible thing to think how happy we are, for it cannot last. Something melancholy is sure to happen to us, and till it comes we shall live in dread of it; for we know happiness never lasts, and this is a thought that makes me very sad.”

===Chinese===
“咱们眼下的幸福，想起来实在可怕，因为它是长不了的。准有一桩伤心事在等着咱们；它一日不来，咱们一日提心吊胆。谁都知道幸福从不长久——这个念头，想想就叫我伤心得很。”

===Original===
The wife had now also taken some bread.

“What is this you are saying?” she said. “How can you think such dreadful things? I do not like you when you talk like that; and I think it is very hard for me to be married to a man who wants to be unhappy.”

===Chinese===
这会儿妻子也吃了面包。

“你这是说的什么话？”她说，“你怎么能想这些可怕的念头？你这样说话的时候，我都不喜欢你了。我寻思着，嫁了一个一心要不痛快的男人，我这命也真是苦得很。”

===Original===
“The best thing we can hope for,” said the husband, sighing, “is for some great misfortune to befall us; then we should be all right, for we should know then, that we knew the worst that could come. As it is we shall live in suspense all our days.”

“Now,” cried his wife, “I am indeed unfortunate. What could be worse than to have a husband who does not like being happy? I wish I had married someone else; or indeed had no husband at all.”

So both began to grumble, and at last to quarrel, and finally both were crying with anger.

===Chinese===
“咱们顶好巴望的，”丈夫叹口气说，“就是有桩大祸快点落到头上。那样倒安生了：最坏的情形既然已知，往后就再没什么可怕。如今这样，咱们可得一辈子悬着心过日子。”

“好哇，”妻子嚷道，“我这可真是苦命人了。嫁了个不肯享福的丈夫，天底下还有比这更糟的事吗？但愿我当初嫁的是别人；索性压根儿没有丈夫，那才好呢。”

于是两个先是嘟囔，继而拌嘴，末了双双气得哭了起来。

===Original===
Not far out of the village was a large pleasant farmhouse, standing amongst fields, and the farmer was a hale, bright man, with a good wife and pretty children. He was very busy just now getting in the corn, for it was autumn, and he stood among his men, directing them as they worked in the fields. He had not had time to have a proper breakfast before going to work, but his wife sent some out to him with some of the baker’s new bread, and he sat down under a tree to eat it. As he did so he looked up at the farmhouse, and thought, with pride, that it was the largest farm in all the country round, and that it had belonged to his father, and his grandfather, and his great-grandfather, before him.

===Chinese===
出村不远，有一座宽敞舒坦的农庄，坐落在田野中间。庄主是个硬朗爽快的人，有个贤惠的妻子和几个俊俏的孩子。眼下正是秋天，他忙着收庄稼，站在雇工们中间，指派他们在田里干活。下地之前，他没工夫好好吃顿早饭，妻子便把饭送到地头，还带着面包师家新出炉的面包；他就坐在一棵树下吃起来。吃着，他抬头望了望那座农舍，得意地想：方圆左近，就数自家这份田产最大；它是父亲、祖父、曾祖父，一代代传到他手上的。

===Original===
“ ’Tis a fine old house, for sure,” thought he, as he took a large piece of bread, “ ’Tis so well built and strong;” but no sooner had he swallowed a mouthful than his thoughts changed.

“What should I do if it were to fall down and crush me some day,” he said to himself. “After all, ’tis only built of brick, and might tumble any day. How much stronger it would have been if it had been built of stone. Then it would not have been nearly so likely to give way. Really when my great-grandfather built it he should have thought of this. How selfish all men are;” and he became quite unhappy lest his house should fall, and lamented while he ate.

===Chinese===
“这老宅子可真够气派的，”他一面想，一面掰下一大块面包，“盖得多结实，多牢靠。”可一口下肚，他的念头就变了。

“哪天它塌下来，把我压在底下，可怎么好？”他自言自语道，“说到底，这宅子不过是砖砌的，说不定哪天就塌了。当初若用石头盖，该结实多少！也就断不至于这么容易塌。我曾祖父盖它的时候，真该想到这一层才是。唉，人哪，个个都这么自私！”他越想越愁，只怕房子塌下来，一面吃，一面唉声叹气。

===Original===
In the kitchen the farmer’s wife was very busy cooking and cleaning, and scarcely stopped to eat till near midday. Then she took up a piece of bread and cheese, and leant against the window as she ate it, that she might watch for her eldest girl and boy, Janey and Jimmy, who would now be returning from school.

===Chinese===
厨房里，农夫的妻子正忙着烧饭刷洗，直到快晌午才得空停下来吃东西。她拿了块面包和干酪，倚着窗口边吃边张望——她家那一双大儿女，珍妮（Janey）和吉米（Jimmy），这会儿该放学回来了。

===Original===
“Our baker really bakes very decent bread,” said she; “ ’tis almost as good as my own;” and she went on eating till she saw her two children coming through the fields together.

“Here they come,” said she; “How bonny they look. Really I ought to be very proud of them. I don’t know which is the prettier, Janey or Jimmy, but ’tis a pity, for sure, that Janey is the eldest. It would be much better if Jimmy were older than she. ’Tis a bad thing for the sister to be older than the brother. Now, if he were her age, and she were his, that would be really nice, for then he could take care of her and see after her; but, as it is, she will try to direct him, and boys never like to obey their sisters; I really almost think I had better not have had any children at all,” and the tears filled her eyes, and when her girl and boy ran in to her, her face was very sad, and she seemed to be scarcely glad to see them.

===Chinese===
“咱们这面包师烤的面包还真不赖，”她说，“快赶上我自个儿烤的了。”她一路吃着，直到望见两个孩子正一道从田野间走来。

“来了，来了，”她说，“瞧他们多水灵。说真的，我本该为他们骄傲才是。珍妮和吉米哪个更俊，我可说不上来；只是可惜呀，偏偏珍妮是姐姐。若吉米比她大，那才叫好。姐姐比弟弟大，可不是桩好事。他俩的年纪若能对换一下，那才叫称心如意——那样弟弟就能照应姐姐、看顾姐姐了。如今呢，倒过来是姐姐要管着弟弟，可男孩子家，从来就不肯听姐姐的。我简直觉得，当初干脆一个孩子也不生才好。”她说着，眼泪涌了上来。等到女儿儿子跑进门扑到她跟前，她满脸愁容，见了他们竟像不怎么欢喜似的。

===Original===
So things went on all over the village. Each one as he tasted the bread grew discontented and angry, till at last all the people went about grumbling and complaining, or else shedding tears outright. Only the baker himself was cheerful and merry, and sang as he kneaded his dough, and sold it to his customers with a light heart, for his trade had never been so good. Every atom of bread he made was sold at once, so he cared not one whit for the trouble of the other people, and laughed to himself when he heard them complaining, and thought of the words of the dark little elf.

===Chinese===
全村都是这般光景。谁尝了那面包，谁就不知足、爱动气；到后来，人人满腹牢骚、见天抱怨，再不就干脆抹起眼泪来。只有面包师自己欢天喜地，一边揉面一边唱歌，心头轻快地把面包卖给主顾——他的买卖从没这么红火过。他做的面包刚出炉就卖个精光，因此旁人的烦恼，他一星半点儿也不放在心上，听见人家抱怨，还暗自好笑，心里想起那个小黑妖说过的话。

===Original===
One day as he stood kneading at the door and whistling to himself, the doctor walked past and looked angrily at him.

“What on earth are you making that whistling for?” he asked. “I declare one would think that you were as happy as a man could be.”

===Chinese===
有一天，他正站在门口揉面，嘴里吹着口哨，医生打门前经过，气冲冲地瞪了他一眼。

“你吹的哪门子口哨？”他问，“说真的，人家还只当你快活得天下第一呢。”

===Original===
“And so I am,” said the baker, “And so I should think were you too, for you have nothing to trouble you.”

“Nothing to trouble me, forsooth!” cried the doctor in a rage. “How dare you insult me in this way? I tell you what it is, my fine fellow, I think you are very impertinent, and if I have any more of your impudence I will take my stick and thrash you soundly. It really is not to be borne, that one man should be allowed to tell another that he has nothing to complain of.”

===Chinese===
“我可不就是嘛，”面包师说，“依我看，你也该快活——你又没什么可烦心的。”

“没什么可烦心的，亏你说得出口！”医生勃然大怒，“你竟敢这样作践我！我把话搁在这儿，好小子：你放肆得很；再敢无礼一句，我就摘下手杖，结结实实抽你一顿。这世上竟容得一个人对另一个人说，他没什么可抱怨的——真真是叫人忍无可忍！”

===Original===
“Nay, *you* can have as much to complain of as you like, so long as I have not,” cried the baker, and he laughed loudly. This only made the doctor angrier still, and he was just going to seize the baker when up came the farmer.

“Was there ever such a village as this?” he cried. “It is not fit for anyone to live in, there is always such fighting and quarrelling going on. What is the matter here?”

===Chinese===
“哪儿的话，*你*爱抱怨多少便抱怨多少，只消我自己没得抱怨就好，”面包师嚷道，随即放声大笑。这一笑更把医生气得七窍生烟，他正要伸手揪面包师，农夫赶了上来。

“天底下可有过这样的村子？”他嚷道，“这地方简直住不得人——成天价打呀吵呀，没个消停。这儿又是怎么回事？”

===Original===
“Matter enough,” cried the doctor. “Here is a fellow dares to tell me I have nothing to complain of, nor he either.”

“This is monstrous!” said the farmer; “he deserves to be hung. How dares he say such a thing on such a wretched day as this, with such a blue sky and such a bright sun?”

===Chinese===
“怎么回事，还用问！”医生嚷道，“有这么个家伙，竟敢说我没什么可抱怨的，还说他自己也没有。”

“真是骇人听闻！”农夫说，“这就该把他绞死才解气。瞧这么个凄惨日子——蓝天莹莹，日头亮堂堂——他怎么敢说这样的话？”

===Original===
“Why, Master Farmer,” cried the baker, “yesterday you grumbled because it was raining, and now you grumble because it is fine.”

“And I tell you that it is enough to make one grumble,” said the farmer. “It should have been fair yesterday, and should have rained today. You ought to be ashamed of such talk, Master Baker, and I think it would serve you justly right if we took you before the Justice and let us see what he thinks of your conduct.”

“Nay!” cried the baker, beginning to be frightened, “what have I done that I am to be taken before the Justice?”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，农夫老哥，”面包师嚷道，“昨儿下雨你抱怨，今儿天晴了，你又抱怨。”

“我告诉你，这还不够叫人抱怨的吗？”农夫说，“昨儿本该放晴，今儿本该下雨，才是正理！你说这话，该害臊，面包师老板。依我看，就该把你扭送到法官大人跟前，瞧瞧他怎么评评你这份行止，那才叫恰如其分。”

“使不得！”面包师叫道，心里发慌了，“我干了什么，要把我扭送到法官大人跟前？”

===Original===
“What have you done, indeed!” said the doctor. “We will see if the Justice cannot find that out pretty quickly.” So they seized the baker and dragged him away in spite of himself, and as they pulled him through the village the people thronged about them, and followed till there was quite a large crowd.

===Chinese===
“你干了什么，问得好！”医生说，“法官大人能不能三两下查个明白，咱们这就去见分晓。”说着，两人便揪住面包师，由不得他分说，一路拖了走。穿过村子时，人们纷纷围拢上来，越聚越多，末了汇成好大一伙人。

===Original===
The Justice sat at his door smoking a pipe, with tears in his eyes.

“Now what is all this uproar for?” cried he. “Am I never to be left in peace? How hard is the life of a Justice!” but he got up and came out on the steps to meet them.

===Chinese===
法官坐在自家门口抽烟斗，眼里噙着泪。

“这又是闹的哪一出？”他嚷道，“就没个叫我安生的时候？法官的日子，可真苦哇！”话虽如此，他还是站起身，迎下台阶来。

===Original===
“See here,” cried the doctor; “here is a man who says he has nothing to complain of, and we have brought him to you, to know if he is to be punished, or to be allowed to go on talking like this.”

“Certainly not,” cried the Justice, “or we shall soon have the whole village in an uproar. Let him be taken to the marketplace, and I will order that he be publicly flogged by the soldiers.”

===Chinese===
“您瞧，”医生嚷道，“这儿有个人，说自己没什么可抱怨的。我们把他带到您跟前，请您发落：是该治他的罪呢，还是容他这么继续满嘴浑说？”

“自然容不得，”法官嚷道，“不然的话，全村很快就要闹个底朝天。把他带到集市上去，我传令叫士兵们当众鞭打他。”

===Original===
At this the poor baker burst out crying, and entreated to be let off, saying that now indeed he had plenty to complain of, but at this the justice was angrier still. “Then,” said he, “you certainly deserve to be flogged for having told an untruth before, when you said you had not. Take him away, and do as I bid.”

===Chinese===
可怜的面包师一听，放声大哭，哀求饶他这一遭，说他如今可真有一大堆可抱怨的了。谁知这话反倒叫法官越发动气。“那么，”他说，“先前你说没有，便是扯了谎；这一顿鞭子，你更挨得活该。带下去，照我的吩咐办。”

===Original===
So they dragged the baker off to the marketplace, and made a ring round him, so that he could not escape, and then there came down two or three soldiers with ropes in their hands, and they seized him, and began to beat him before all the crowd.

===Chinese===
于是众人把面包师拖到集市上，围成一个大圈，教他插翅难逃。随后来了两三个士兵，手里拿着绳索，揪住他，当着满集的人群抽打起来。

===Original===
But by this time all the people were so enraged against him, that a number of them cried, “Let us go to his house and pull it down.” So off they ran to the baker’s house, and broke the windows and knocked about the furniture, and then some of them fell on the oven, and wrenched off the door, and others seized the pokers and tongs, and smashed in its sides, and in the hurry and scuffle, the little dark man crept out of the oven and scuttled away unseen by anyone. But no sooner had he gone than a great change came across the people.

===Chinese===
可到这时，众人对面包师怒不可遏，有一伙人喊道：“走，上他家去，把房子拆了！”于是他们一窝蜂跑到面包师家，砸破窗子，摔打家具；接着有些人扑到烤炉上，拧下炉门，另一些人抄起火钩火钳，把炉身两壁砸得稀烂。慌乱推搡之间，那个小黑妖从炉子里溜了出来，一转身窜得没了影儿，谁也不曾瞧见。可他前脚刚走，众人便大大变了样。

===Original===
The soldiers on the green stopped beating the baker, and looked at each other aghast, and the Justice called out,

“Stop! What is all this uproar about? And what has this man done that you are beating him without my orders?” and the people in the crowd whispered to each other; “It is true—what has he done?” and they slunk away, looking ashamed.

===Chinese===
草地上，士兵们住了手，不再打面包师，面面相觑，满脸惊惶；法官喊道：

“住手！这是闹的什么？我并没下过令，你们凭什么打这个人？”人群里的人交头接耳：“可不是嘛——他到底干了什么来着？”说罢，一个个面带愧色，溜之大吉。

===Original===
The Justice also at first looked somewhat ashamed of himself, but he drew himself up, and looking very important, said, “There, my man, you are forgiven for this once, and now go your way, and see that you behave better in future;” and then he walked away with much dignity.

So the baker was left alone in the marketplace, and he cried for rage and pain.

===Chinese===
法官起先也有几分讪讪，随即挺直身子，端足了架子说：“行了，汉子，这回且饶了你。去吧，往后放规矩点儿。”说完便迈着方步，好不威严地走了。

集市上只剩下面包师独自一个，他又气又痛，放声大哭。

===Original===
“This all comes of the oven imp,” cried he, as he limped home. “Directly I get home I will drive him out of my oven, and away from my house. Better to have a hundred batches of bread spoiled than to be flogged for saying one is happy.” But when he reached his house the little dark man was nowhere to be found; there was nought but the broken oven with its sides battered in.

===Chinese===
“这都是炉子里的小黑妖惹出来的，”他一路瘸着往家走，一路哭喊，“一进家门，我就要把他从我的炉子里赶出去，撵出我的屋子！宁可一百炉面包全烤糊，也强似因为说了句自己快活，就挨一顿鞭子。”可他到了家，那小黑妖早已没了踪影，只剩一座破炉子，两壁砸得坑坑洼洼。

===Original===
The baker mended the oven, and from that time forth his bread was just like other people’s; but for all that he had learnt to be quite contented, for now he knew that there were worse things than having his loaves burnt black, and he was only too well pleased to take his chance with other people, without the help of fairy folk. As for the little black imp, he was never heard of more, and the people in the village soon recovered their good humour, and were just as happy and contented as they had been before they tasted the bread of discontent.

===Chinese===
面包师把炉子修补好了；打那以后，他烤的面包便同旁人的一样。虽说如此，他倒学会了心满意足——如今他知道，世上有比面包烤糊糟糕得多的事；不必仙灵帮衬，同大伙儿一样听天由命，他已觉千好万好。至于那个小黑妖，从此再没有人听说过他。村里人的好兴致不久也回来了，又像尝到那不满面包（the Bread of Discontent）之前一样，快快活活，心满意足。

## The Three Clever Kings / 三个聪明国王

===Original===
Old King Roland lay upon his deathbed, and as he had no son to reign after him he sent for his three nephews, Aldovrand, Aldebert, and Alderete, and addressed them as follows:—

===Chinese===
老国王罗兰（Roland）躺在临终的病榻上。他身后没有儿子继位，便把三个侄儿召到跟前——阿尔多夫兰德（Aldovrand）、阿尔德贝特（Aldebert）和阿尔德雷特（Alderete）——对他们说道：

===Original===
“My dear nephews, I feel that my days are now drawing to an end, and one of you will have to be King when I am dead. But there is no pleasure in being King. My people have been difficult to govern and never content with what I did for them, so that my life has been a hard one, and though I have watched you all closely, still I know not, which is most fit to wear the crown; so my wish is that you should each try it in turn. You, Aldovrand, as you are the oldest, shall be King first, and if you reign happily, all well and good; but if you fail, let Aldebert take your place; and if he fail, let him give it up to Alderete, and then you will know which is the best fitted to govern.”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的侄儿们，我觉着我的日子快要到头了。我死之后，你们当中总得有一个来做国王。可是做国王实在没什么乐趣。我的子民难于治理，我为他们做的一切，他们从来不曾满意，因此我这一生过得辛苦。你们三个，我都细细看在眼里，可到底谁最配戴这顶王冠，我仍拿不准。所以我愿你们轮流试上一试。阿尔多夫兰德，你是老大，先由你做国王；你若治得快活，那就再好不过；你若不行，就让阿尔德贝特接替；他若也不行，就再让给阿尔德雷特。到那时，你们就知道谁最适合治理这个国家了。”

===Original===
On this the three young men all thanked their uncle, and each one declared that he would do his best, and soon after old King Roland died and was buried with great state and ceremony.

So now Aldovrand was to be King, and he was crowned, and there were great rejoicings everywhere.

===Chinese===
三个年轻人听了，都向伯父道谢，个个表示定当竭尽全力。不久，老国王罗兰驾崩，以极隆重的典礼下了葬。

于是轮到阿尔多夫兰德做国王了。他行了加冕礼，举国上下一片欢腾。

===Original===
“ ’Tis a fine thing to be King,” cried he in much glee; “Now I can amuse myself and do just as I please, and there will be no one to stop me, and I will lie in bed as late as I like in the morning, for who dares blame one, if one is King?”

===Chinese===
“做国王可真美，”他乐得直嚷，“如今我想怎么玩就怎么玩，爱做什么就做什么，再没有人拦得住我。早晨我爱赖到多晚便赖到多晚——一个人做了国王，谁敢说半个不字？”

===Original===
Next morning the Prime Minister and the Chancellor came to the palace to see the new King and settle affairs of state, but they were told that his majesty was in bed and had given orders that no one should disturb him.

===Chinese===
第二天一早，首相（the Prime Minister）和宰相（the Chancellor）进宫求见新王，要商定国事，却听说陛下还没起床，并且传下话来，任何人不得打扰。

===Original===
“This is a bad beginning,” sighed the Prime Minister.

“Very bad,” echoed the Chancellor.

===Chinese===
“这可不是个好开头，”首相叹道。

“坏得很哪，”宰相随声附和。

===Original===
When they came back to the palace later in the day the King was playing at battledore and shuttlecock with some of his gentlemen, and was very angry at being interrupted in his game.

“A pretty thing,” he cried, “that I the King am to be sent for hither and thither as if I were a lackey. They must go away and come another time;” and on hearing this the Prime Minister and Chancellor looked graver still.

===Chinese===
当天晚些时候他们再回宫里，国王正同几位廷臣打板羽球，玩法正酣，被人搅了局，好不恼火。

“好嘛，”他嚷道，“我堂堂国王，竟给人召来唤去，倒像我是个听差小厮似的！叫他们走，改日再来。”首相和宰相听了这话，脸色越发凝重。

===Original===
But next morning there came the Commander-in-Chief and the Lord High Admiral, as well as the Prime Minister and the Chancellor, all wanting to have an audience with the King, and as he was not out of bed and they could not wait any longer, they all stood outside his bedroom door, and knocked to gain admittance, and at last he came out in a towering rage, and throwing them his crown, cried,

===Chinese===
可是第二天早上，总司令（the Commander-in-Chief）和海军大臣（the Lord High Admiral）也来了，同首相、宰相一道，全都要面见国王。国王还没起床，他们又等不得了，便一齐站在寝宫门外，敲门求见。国王终于出来了，怒气冲天，把王冠朝他们一扔，喊道：

===Original===
“Here, let one of my cousins be King, for I will not bear this longer. It is much more trouble than it is worth, so Aldebert or Alderete may try it and see how they like it, but as for me, I have had enough of it,” and he ran downstairs and out of the palace door, leaving the Prime Minister and the Chancellor and the General and Admiral staring at each other in dismay.

===Chinese===
“拿去，叫我哪个堂弟来做国王吧，这差事我再也受不了啦！操心受累，实在不值——阿尔德贝特也好，阿尔德雷特也好，谁来试试尝尝滋味。至于我嘛，可受够了。”说完他奔下楼梯，冲出宫门，撇下首相、宰相和将军、海军大臣，四个人面面相觑，惊惶失色。

===Original===
Aldovrand walked out of the town unnoticed, and turned towards the country, whistling cheerily to himself. When he had gone some way in the fields, he came to a farmhouse, and in a meadow near, the farmer stood talking to his men. Aldovrand went straight up to him, and, touching his hat, asked if he could give him any work.

“Work?” cried the farmer, little thinking he was talking to his late king. “Why, what sort of work can you do?”

===Chinese===
阿尔多夫兰德神不知鬼不觉地出了城，朝乡下走去，一路欢欢喜喜吹着口哨。在田野间走了好一程，他来到一座农庄；近旁的草地上，农夫正站在雇工中间说话。阿尔多夫兰德径直走上前去，碰了碰帽檐，问他可有活计给自己做。

“活计？”农夫叫道，万万想不到自己说话的对象正是方才逊位的国王，“嗬，你能干什么活呀？”

===Original===
“Well,” said Aldovrand, “I am not very fond of running about, but if you want anyone to mind your sheep, or keep the birds from your corn, I could do that nicely.”

“I tell you what you can do if you like,” said the farmer. “I am wanting a goose-boy to take care of my geese. See, there they are on the common. All you will have to do is to see that they don’t stray away, and to drive them in at night.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，”阿尔多夫兰德说，“我不大爱跑动。可你若要人看羊，或是赶田里的鸟儿，这活儿我保管干得漂亮。”

“我告诉你个合适的活计，”农夫说，“我正缺个放鹅娃，替我看鹅。瞧，鹅就在那片公地上。你只消看住它们别走散，晚上赶回圈里去就成。”

===Original===
“That will suit me exactly,” cried Aldovrand. “I will begin at once;” and he went straight on to the common, and when he had collected the geese together lay down to watch them in high good humour.

“This is capital,” he cried, “and much better than being King at the palace. Here there is no Prime Minister or Chancellor to come worrying;” and he lay watching the geese all day very contentedly.

===Chinese===
“这活儿正合我的心意！”阿尔多夫兰德嚷道，“我这就开工。”说罢他径直走到公地上，把鹅拢在一处，便乐悠悠地躺下看起鹅来。

“真是再妙不过，”他嚷道，“比在王宫里做国王强多啦。这儿没有首相宰相来聒噪烦人。”他躺着看了一整天鹅，心里说不出的满足。

===Original===
When the Prime Minister and the Chancellor knew that Aldovrand was really gone, they went in a great hurry to Aldebert to tell him that it was his turn to be King. But when he heard how his cousin had run away, he looked frightened.

“I will do my best,” quoth he; “but I really know very little about the matter. However, you must tell me, and I will do whatever you direct.”

===Chinese===
首相和宰相打听到阿尔多夫兰德是真的走了，便急忙去找阿尔德贝特，告诉他这回轮到他做国王了。可阿尔德贝特听说堂兄怎么跑掉的，脸上露出害怕的神色。

“我自当竭尽全力，”他道，“可这事我实在懂得很少。这样吧，你们得随时指点我，你们怎么说，我怎么做便是。”

===Original===
At hearing this the Prime Minister and the Chancellor were delighted.

“Now we have got the right sort of King,” they said; and both wagged their heads with joy.

So King Aldebert was crowned, and there were great rejoicings all over the country.

===Chinese===
一听这话，首相和宰相心花怒放。

“这回我们可算遇到对的国王了，”两人说道，乐得直点头。

于是阿尔德贝特国王加了冕，举国上下一片欢腾。

===Original===
Early next morning he was up all ready to receive his Ministers, and first came the Prime Minister.

“Your Majesty,” said he, “I come to you on an affair of much importance. A great part of our city is falling down, and it is very necessary that we should rebuild it at once. If you will command it, therefore, I will see that it is done.”

===Chinese===
第二天一早他就起身，齐齐整整地预备接见各位大臣；头一个来的是首相。

“陛下，”他说，“我为一桩要紧事而来。咱们京城有一大片房子眼看要塌了，非得马上重建不可。只要陛下降旨，我保管办妥。”

===Original===
“I have no doubt you are right,” said the King; “pray let them begin building at once;” and the Prime Minister went away delighted.

Scarcely had he gone when in came the Commander-in-Chief.

===Chinese===
“想必你说得不错，”国王说，“那就劳你大驾，叫他们即刻动工吧。”首相欢天喜地地走了。

他前脚刚走，总司令后脚就进来了。

===Original===
“Your Majesty,” said he, “I wish to lay before you the state of our army. Our soldiers have had a great deal of fighting to do lately, and are beginning to be discontented, but the late King, your uncle, would never attend to their wants.”

“Pray do what you like,” said King Aldebert.

===Chinese===
“陛下，”他说，“我想把咱们军队的情形禀报给您。士兵们近来仗打得太多，已开始心怀不满；可先王，您的伯父，从来不管他们的诉求。”

“请你只管看着办，”阿尔德贝特国王说。

===Original===
“To satisfy them,” said the Commander-in-Chief, “I think that we should double their pay. This would keep them in a good humour, and all will go well.”

“By all means, that will certainly be the best way,” said Aldebert. “Let it be given to them at once;” and on hearing this, the Commander-in-Chief went away right merrily.

===Chinese===
“要安抚他们，”总司令说，“依我看，该把他们的饷银加倍。这样一来他们心气就顺了，什么事都好办。”

“务必照办，这自然是顶好的法子，”阿尔德贝特说，“立刻发给他们吧。”总司令听了，乐呵呵地走了。

===Original===
When he had gone, there came in the Chancellor with a long face.

“Your Majesty,” he said, “I have this morning been to the treasury, and I find that there is scarcely any money left. The late King, your uncle, spent so much in spite of all I could say, that now it is almost all gone. Your Majesty must now save all you can for the next year or two, and you ought also to lower the soldiers’ pay, and stop all public works.”

===Chinese===
他走后，宰相板着一张长脸进来了。

“陛下，”他说，“今早我去了一趟国库，发现里头几乎一个钱也不剩了。先王，您伯父，不管我怎么劝，花钱如流水，如今库底子快见空了。往后这一两年，陛下务必处处节省，还应当削减士兵的饷银，停掉一切公共工程。”

===Original===
“I have no doubt you are quite right,” cried the King. “You know best, let it be done as you wish.”

But next morning in came the Prime Minister with a frowning face. “How is this, your Majesty?” cried he. “Just as we are beginning our buildings, the Chancellor comes and tells us that we are not to have any money to build with.” He had not done speaking when the Commander-in-Chief burst into the room unable to conceal his rage.

===Chinese===
“想必你说得很对，”国王叫道，“你顶清楚，就照你的意思办吧。”

可是第二天早上，首相皱着眉头闯了进来。“这是怎么回事，陛下？”他嚷道，“我们这边房子刚动工，宰相就来说，建房的款项一个也没有了。”话音未落，总司令也冲进屋来，压不住一腔怒火。

===Original===
“Yesterday your Majesty told me that all the soldiers should have double pay, and this morning I hear, that instead of that, their wages are to be lowered!” Here he was interrupted by the Chancellor, who came running in looking much excited,

“Your Majesty,” he cried, “did you not yesterday say we were now to begin saving, and that I was not to allow any more money to be spent, and that the army must do with less pay?”

===Chinese===
“昨天陛下亲口对我说，士兵们一律加双饷；今儿一早却听说，饷银不但不加，反倒要削减！”话没说完，宰相也奔了进来，满脸激动，把他打断了：

“陛下，”他嚷道，“您昨天不是说要开始节省，叫我一个钱也不许再花，军队也必须少领些饷吗？”

===Original===
And then all three began to quarrel among themselves. When he saw how angry they were, King Aldebert took off his crown and said, “I am sure you are each of you quite right; but I think I am scarcely fit to be a King. Indeed I think you had better find my cousin Alderete, and let him be crowned, and I will seek my fortune elsewhere.” And he had slipped out of the room, and run downstairs and out of the palace, before they could stop him.

===Chinese===
于是三个人争吵起来。阿尔德贝特国王眼看他们怒气冲冲，便摘下王冠，说道：“我敢保你们每一位都对；只是我看，我实在不大配做国王。说真的，你们不如去把我堂弟阿尔德雷特找来，让他加冕，我要到别处去碰碰自己的运气。”趁他们还没回过神，他已溜出房间，奔下楼梯，跑出宫门去了。

===Original===
He went briskly down the highroad into the country, the same way that Aldovrand had gone.

After he had gone some way, he met a travelling tinker who sat by the roadside mending tin cans, with his little fire at his side.

===Chinese===
他脚步轻快，沿着大路进了乡间——正是阿尔多夫兰德出走的那条道。

走了一程，他遇见一个走街串巷的锡匠（tinker），坐在路边修补锡壶锡罐，身旁燃着一小炉火。

===Original===
Aldebert stood watching him, and at last said, “How cleverly you mend those holes! You must lead a pleasant life, going from house to house in the green lanes mending wares. Do you think I could learn how to do it if you would teach me?”

The tinker, who was an old man, looked at him and said,

===Chinese===
阿尔德贝特站在一旁看他干活，末了说道：“你这洞补得多巧啊！沿着绿荫小巷走家串户、修补器皿，这日子一定过得惬意。你可肯教我？你说我学得会吗？”

锡匠是位老人，打量了他一眼，说道：

===Original===
“Well, I don’t mind giving you a trial if you like to come with me, for I want a strong young man sometimes to help me wheel my little cart, and I’ll teach you my trade, and we’ll see what you can make of it.”

So Aldebert was delighted, and went with the tinker.

===Chinese===
“行啊，你若愿意跟我走，我不妨叫你试一试。我正缺个壮实的小伙子，时不时帮我推推小车。我把手艺教给你，瞧瞧你能学出个什么名堂。”

阿尔德贝特喜出望外，便跟着锡匠走了。

===Original===
When they knew he was really gone the Prime Minister and the Chancellor looked at each other in dismay.

“This will never do,” cried they; “we must go at once to Prince Alderete; and let us hope he may do better than his cousins.”

===Chinese===
待打听到他真的走了，首相和宰相面面相觑，一筹莫展。

“这样下去可不行，”他们嚷道，“得马上找阿尔德雷特王子去。但愿他比两位堂兄争气些。”

===Original===
When Prince Alderete heard that it was his turn to reign he jumped for joy.

“Now,” cried he, “at last I will show what a king should really be like. My cousins were neither of them any good, but they shall now see how different I will be.”

===Chinese===
阿尔德雷特王子听说轮到自己登基，高兴得跳了起来。

“好啊，”他嚷道，“这回我总算能露一手，让大家瞧瞧真正的国王该是什么样！我那两个堂兄没一个顶用的，如今他们且看看，我跟他们有多大分别。”

===Original===
So he was crowned, and again there were great rejoicings all over the country.

Next day he sat in state to receive the Chancellor and Prime Minister and hear what they had to say.

===Chinese===
于是他加了冕，举国上下又是一片欢腾。

第二天，他端坐御座，一本正经地接见宰相和首相，听他们奏事。

===Original===
“My friends,” said he to them, “a good King ought to be like a father to his people, and this is what I mean to be. I mean to arrange everything for them myself, and if they will only obey me, and do as I direct, they are sure to be both prosperous and happy.”

On hearing this both Prime Minister and Chancellor looked anxious, and the Chancellor said,

===Chinese===
“二位朋友，”他对他们说，“一位好国王，对子民就该像父亲一样；我正要做这样的国王。样样事情我都要亲自替他们安排好。他们只要乖乖服从我、照我说的做，保管又兴旺又幸福。”

一听这话，首相和宰相都露出愁容，宰相说：

===Original===
“I fear, your Majesty, your people will not like to be too much meddled with.” At this the King was very angry, and bid them see about their own business, and not presume to teach him his.

When they had gone he went to take a drive in his city, that he might see it and know it well; but directly he returned to the palace he sent for the Prime Minister, and when he had arrived, said,

===Chinese===
“只怕，陛下，您的子民不喜欢事事都受人摆布。”这话惹得国王大怒，喝令他们管好自己的差事，休得放肆来教训他。

他们退下之后，他乘车到城里兜了一圈，要把京城看个仔细、记个烂熟；可他一回到宫里，立刻召来首相，等人到了，便说：

===Original===
“I already see much to be altered in my kingdom. I do not like the houses in which many of the people dwell, nor indeed the dresses they wear; but what strikes me most of all is, that wherever I go I smell a strong smell of pea soup. Now, nothing is so unwholesome as pea soup, and therefore it would not be right in me to allow the people to go on eating it. I command, therefore, that no one shall again make, or eat pea soup, within my realm on pain of death.”

===Chinese===
“我的王国里，我已经看出许许多多要改的地方。许多百姓住的房子，我不喜欢；他们身上穿的衣裳，我也不喜欢。可最叫我吃惊的是：不管走到哪儿，我都闻到一股浓烈的豌豆汤味儿。要知道，天下再没有比豌豆汤更伤身子的东西了，所以，若容百姓继续喝下去，那就是我的不是。因此我下令：从今往后，我的国境之内，谁也不得再熬豌豆汤、喝豌豆汤，违者处死！”

===Original===
Again the Prime Minister looked very grave, and began to say,

“Your Majesty, your subjects will surely not like to be hindered from eating and drinking what pleases them!” But the King cried out in a rage,

“Go at once and do as I bid you.” So the Prime Minister had to obey.

===Chinese===
首相又摆出满脸凝重，开口说：

“陛下，爱吃爱喝的东西也叫禁止，您的臣民们必定不肯依的！”国王勃然大怒，喝道：

“立刻照我的吩咐去办！”首相只好领命。

===Original===
Early next morning when the King arose he heard a great hubbub under his window, and when he went to see what it was, he saw a vast mob of people all shouting, “The King, the King! Where is this King who would dictate to us what we shall eat and drink?”

When he saw them he was terribly frightened, and at once sent off for the Prime Minister and Chancellor to come to his aid.

===Chinese===
第二天清早，国王刚起身，就听得窗下一片喧哗。他过去一看，只见黑压压一大群人，齐声喊：“国王！国王！那个管我们吃、管我们喝的国王在哪儿？”

他一见这阵势，吓得魂飞魄散，连忙派人去请首相和宰相来搭救。

===Original===
“Pray go and tell them to eat what they like,” he cried when they arrived; “But, do you know, I find it will not at all suit me to be King. You had best try Aldovrand, or Aldebert, again;” and, so saying, he took off his crown and laid it down, and slipped away out of the palace before either Prime Minister or Chancellor could stop him.

===Chinese===
“求你们快去告诉他们，爱吃什么吃什么！”两人一到他就嚷，“不过，你们瞧，我算是明白了，做国王这差事压根儿不合我的脾胃。你们还是再去请阿尔多夫兰德，或者阿尔德贝特吧。”说着，他摘下王冠，往那儿一放，趁首相宰相还没来得及拦，溜出宫去了。

===Original===
He went out of the back door, and ran, and ran, and ran, till he had left the town far behind, and came to the country fields and lanes—the same way that his two cousins had gone; and as he went he met a sweep trudging along carrying his long brooms over his shoulder.

“My friend,” cried Alderete, stopping him, “Of all things in the world I should like to be a sweep and learn how to sweep chimneys. May I go with you, and will you teach me your trade?”

===Chinese===
他从后门出去，跑啊，跑啊，跑啊，直跑得把城镇远远抛在身后，来到乡间的田野和小路上——正是他两位堂兄走过的那条道。走着走着，他遇见一个扫烟囱的（sweep），肩上扛着长把儿扫帚，一路跋涉。

“我的朋友，”阿尔德雷特拦住他，喊道，“天底下的事，我就想当个扫烟囱的，学学怎么扫烟囱。我能跟你走吗？你肯把手艺教给我吗？”

===Original===
The sweep looked surprised, but said, “Yes, Alderete could go with him if he chose, and as he was now going on to the farmhouses, on the road, to sweep the chimneys, he could begin at once.” So Alderete went with the sweep, carrying some of his brooms for him.

===Chinese===
扫烟囱的满脸讶异，可还是说：“行啊，阿尔德雷特愿意的话就跟他走；他这会儿正要沿路去各家农舍扫烟囱，阿尔德雷特马上就能上手。”于是阿尔德雷特跟扫烟囱的走了，还替他扛了几把扫帚。

===Original===
After a time the people outside the palace grew quiet, when they heard that the King would not interfere with them further. And when all was again still, the Prime Minister and Chancellor went to seek the King, but he was nowhere to be found in the palace.

“This will never do,” cried they. “We must have a King somehow, so we had best have back one of the others.” So they started to look for Aldovrand or Aldebert.

===Chinese===
过了一阵，宫外的人们听说国王不再来管他们，便渐渐安静下来。等一切重归平静，首相和宰相去寻国王，宫里却哪儿也找不着他。

“这样可不行，”他们嚷道，“国王总得有一个，眼下只好把先前那两位请一位回来。”于是他们动身去找阿尔多夫兰德或阿尔德贝特。

===Original===
They sought them all over the city, and at last they came into the same country road down which the three cousins had gone, and there they saw Aldovrand lying in a meadow watching his flock of geese.

“Good day, my friends,” cried he when he saw them; “And how are things going on at the palace? I hope my cousins like reigning better than I did. Now, here I lie peacefully all day long and watch my geese, and it is much nicer than being King.”

===Chinese===
他们在城里找了个遍，末了走上三堂兄弟先后出走的那条乡路，只见阿尔多夫兰德正躺在草地上，看顾着他那群鹅。

“你们好啊，二位朋友，”他一见他们便嚷道，“宫里这阵子怎么样？但愿我那两个堂兄弟做国王做得比我舒坦。瞧我，成天安安静静躺在这儿看鹅，可比做国王美多啦。”

===Original===
Then the Prime Minister and Chancellor told him all that had happened, and begged that he would come back with them to the palace again, but at this Aldovrand laughed outright.

“No indeed!” cried he, “I would not be King again for any man living. You had best go and seek my cousin Aldebert, and ask him. I saw him go down the road with a tinker, helping him to mend his tins. So go and ask him, and leave me to mind my geese in peace.”

===Chinese===
首相和宰相把事情原原本本说给他听，恳求他随他们回宫再去登基，阿尔多夫兰德听了放声大笑。

“才不呢！”他嚷道，“天下随便什么人来说情，我也绝不再做国王了！你们顶好去寻我堂弟阿尔德贝特，问问他。我瞧见他跟着一个锡匠沿这条路走了，还帮着修补锡器呢。去找他吧，让我安安静静看我的鹅。”

===Original===
So the Prime Minister, and the Chancellor had to seek still farther.

They trudged on and on, till at last they met Aldebert, who sat by the side of the road mending a tin kettle, and whistling cheerily.

===Chinese===
首相和宰相只好继续往前找。

他们一路跋涉，走啊走啊，终于遇见阿尔德贝特——他正坐在路边修补一只锡水壶，嘴里快活地吹着口哨。

===Original===
“Heyday, whom have we here?” cried he. “The Prime Minister and the Chancellor! And I am right glad to see you both. See how clever I have grown; I am learning to be a tinker, and I mended that hole all myself.”

Then the Prime Minister and Chancellor begged him to leave his pots, and come back to the palace and be King, but he fell to work again, harder than ever, and said,

===Chinese===
“嗬，这是哪两位呀？”他嚷道，“首相和宰相！见到你们俩真叫人高兴。瞧我变得多能干——我正学做锡匠呢，那个洞就全是我自个儿补的。”

首相和宰相恳求他撂下锅壶家什，回宫去做国王，他却埋头干起活来，比先前更卖力气，一边说：

===Original===
“No indeed; go and ask my cousins, who are both much cleverer than I. I really don’t do for it at all, but I make a very good tinker, and I like that much better.”

“Then what can we do?” cried the Prime Minister, “for we don’t know where Alderete has gone.”

===Chinese===
“才不呢。去问我那两个堂兄弟吧，他们都比我聪明得多。做国王我实在一窍不通，可锡匠我倒当得满像样，而且我喜欢这行当得多啦。”

“那我们可怎么办呢？”首相嚷道，“阿尔德雷特去了哪儿，我们也不知道哇。”

===Original===
“I saw him go by here with a sweep a little time ago,” said Aldebert; “and he went into that farmhouse yonder, so you had best seek him there.”

So the Prime Minister and the Chancellor went on to the farmhouse. At the door stood the farmer’s wife, but when they asked her if she had seen the King go by, she stared with surprise.

===Chinese===
“方才我见他跟一个扫烟囱的打这儿路过，”阿尔德贝特说，“进了那边那座农舍，你们顶好上那儿寻他去。”

于是首相和宰相朝那座农舍走去。门口站着农夫的妻子，可他们问她可曾见国王打这儿经过，她一脸惊愕。

===Original===
“Nay,” said she; “no one has been here but our sweep and his apprentice. He is in there sweeping the chimney now.” On hearing this, the Prime Minister and Chancellor at once ran into the farmhouse, and saw the old sweep standing by the kitchen fireplace. “And where is the other sweep?” cried they. “He is gone up the chimney, and is just going to begin sweeping,” said the old man. “So if you want to speak to him you must shout.” So they shouted and called,

===Chinese===
“哪儿的话，”她说，“打这儿过的，只有我们家扫烟囱的师傅和他的徒弟。他这会儿正在里头扫烟囱呢。”二人一听，急忙奔进农舍，只见老扫烟囱的站在厨房壁炉旁。“另一位扫烟囱的在哪里？”他们嚷道。“他钻进烟囱里去了，正要动手扫呢，”老人说，“你们要跟他说话，可得大声喊。”于是他们扯开嗓子喊：

===Original===
“King Alderete, King Alderete!” as loud as ever they could, but he did not hear. Then the Chancellor knelt in front of the grate, and put his head up the chimney, and called,

“King Alderete, King Alderete! It is the Prime Minister and I, the Chancellor, come to fetch your Majesty back to the palace.”

===Chinese===
“阿尔德雷特国王！阿尔德雷特国王！”他们能喊多响喊多响，他却听不见。宰相便跪到炉栅跟前，把脑袋探进烟囱，喊道：

“阿尔德雷特国王！阿尔德雷特国王！是首相和我——宰相我——来接陛下回宫的。”

===Original===
When Alderete heard him up the chimney, he trembled in every limb, but he replied,

“I’m not going to come down; I don’t want to be King. I am going to be a sweep, and I like that much better. I shan’t come down till you are gone away, and now you had best go quickly, for I am going to begin sweeping, and all the soot will fall on your head,” and then they heard the rattle of the broom in the chimney, and a whole shower of soot fell on the Chancellor’s head.

===Chinese===
阿尔德雷特在烟囱里听见他的声音，吓得四肢直打颤，可他还是回话说：

“我不下去！我不想做国王。我要当个扫烟囱的，这行当我喜欢得多啦。你们不走，我就不下来。这会儿你们顶好快些走，因为我马上要动手扫了，煤烟灰要全都落在你们脑袋上喽。”话音刚落，只听烟囱里扫帚刷刷作响，一整阵雨似的煤灰，正正落在宰相头上。

===Original===
The Prime Minister and the Chancellor turned back to the city very disconsolately. “We must go and look for a King elsewhere,” they said. “It is no use troubling about Aldovrand, Aldebert, and Alderete.” So they left the one to his geese, and one to his tins, and the other to sweep chimneys, and that was the end of the three clever Kings.

===Chinese===
首相和宰相灰心丧气地折回城里。“我们只好到别处再寻一位国王了，”他们说，“阿尔多夫兰德、阿尔德贝特、阿尔德雷特，这三位是犯不着再费心了。”于是他们由着这一位看他的鹅，那一位补他的锡器，另一位扫他的烟囱——三个聪明国王的故事，就此收场。

## The Wise Princess / 聪明的公主

===Original===
Once upon a time lived a King whose wife was dead and who had one little daughter who was named Fernanda. She was very good and pretty, but when she was a child she vexed all her ladies by asking them questions about everything she saw.

===Chinese===
从前有一位国王，王后早已亡故，膝下只留下一个小女儿，名唤费尔南达（Fernanda）。她心地善良，模样俊俏，可还是个孩子的时候，就见什么问什么，把身边的侍女们问得个个发愁。

===Original===
“Your Highness should not wish to know too much,” they told her, whereat Princess Fernanda threw up her little head, and said,

“I want to know everything.”

===Chinese===
“殿下可不该想知道得太多。”她们这样劝她。费尔南达公主听了，把小脑袋一扬，说道：

“我什么都想知道。”

===Original===
As she grew up she had masters and mistresses to teach her, and learnt every language and every science; but still she said, “It is not enough; I want to know more.”

===Chinese===
她渐渐长大，宫里为她延请了各科男女教师；她学遍了各种语言、各门学问，却仍旧说：“这还不够，我还要知道更多。”

===Original===
In a deep cave underground there lived an old Wizard who was so wise that his face was well-nigh black with wrinkles, and his long white beard flowed to his feet. He knew all sorts of magic, and every day and night sat poring over his books till now there seemed to be nothing left for him to learn.

===Chinese===
地底深处的一个洞穴里，住着一位老巫师。他智慧渊深，一张脸叫皱纹几乎积成了黑色，长长的白须飘飘垂到脚边。他懂得种种巫术魔法，日日夜夜伏在他的书本上苦读，到如今，天底下仿佛再没有什么留给他学的了。

===Original===
One night after everyone was asleep, Princess Fernanda rose and slipped softly down the stairs and out of the palace unheard by anyone, and stole away to the Wizard’s cave.

The old man was sitting on his low stool reading out of an immense book by a dim green light, but he raised his eyes as the Princess entered at the low doorway, and looked at her. She wore a blue and silver robe, but her bright hair was unbound, and fell in ripples to her waist.

===Chinese===
一天夜里，待众人都沉沉睡去，费尔南达公主起了身，轻手轻脚溜下楼梯，悄没声息地出了宫殿，偷偷来到巫师的洞穴。

老人正坐在一张矮凳上，就着幽幽的绿光，读着一本硕大无朋的书；公主从低矮的洞门走进来，他抬起眼睛，把她打量了一番。她穿一身蓝银色的长袍，明亮的头发却披散着，波浪似的一直泻到腰间。

===Original===
“Who are you, and what do you want with me?” he asked shortly.

“I am the Princess Fernanda,” she said, “and I wish to be your pupil. Teach me all you know.”

===Chinese===
“你是谁？找我做什么？”他简慢地问。

“我是费尔南达公主，”她说，“我想做你的学生。把你所知道的，都教给我吧。”

===Original===
“Why do you wish for that?” said the Wizard: “you will not be better or happier for it.”

“I am not happy now,” said the Princess sighing wearily. “Teach me and you shall find me an apt pupil, and I will pay you with gold.”

“I will not have your gold,” said the Wizard, “but come to me every night at this hour, and in three years you shall know all I do.”

===Chinese===
“你为什么要这样？”巫师说，“学了它，你不会变得更好，也不会更快乐。”

“我如今就不快乐，”公主疲惫地叹息道，“教我吧，你会发现我是个颖悟的学生，我要用金币来酬谢你。”

“你的金币我不要，”巫师说，“可你每晚这个时辰到我这里来，三年之后，我所知道的你就全知道了。”

===Original===
So every night the Princess went down to the Wizard’s cave while all the court were sleeping. And the people wondered at her more and more, and said, “How much she knows! How wise she is!”

When the three years had gone by the Wizard said to her, “Go! I can teach you no more now. You are as wise as I.” Then the Princess thanked him and went back to her father’s palace.

===Chinese===
于是每天夜里，趁满朝上下都在酣睡，公主便下到巫师的洞穴里去。人们对她越来越惊叹，都说：“她懂得真多啊！她多聪明啊！”

三年过去，巫师对她说：“去吧！我如今再没有什么可教你的了。你已经同我一样聪明了。”公主谢过了他，回到父亲的王宫里去。

===Original===
She was very wise. She knew the languages of all animals. The fishes came from the deep at her call, and the birds from the trees. She could tell when the winds would rise, and when the sea would be still. She could have turned her enemies to stone, or given untold wealth to her friends. But for all that, when she smiled, her lips were very sad, and her eyes were always full of care. She said she was weary, and her father thought she was sick, and would have sent for the physicians, but she stopped him.

===Chinese===
她真是极聪明的。她通晓一切动物的语言：她一召唤，鱼儿便从深处游来，鸟儿便从树上飞来。她说得出风几时起、海几时静；她若愿意，能把仇敌变成石头，也能给朋友数不清的财富。可是尽管这样，她微笑的时候，唇边总带着深深的哀愁，眼睛里总是盛满了忧思。她说她倦了；父王只当她是病了，要传太医来，却被她拦住了。

===Original===
“How should physicians help me, my father,” she said, “seeing that I know more than they?”

===Chinese===
“太医们哪里帮得上我呢，父王，”她说，“我懂得的比他们还多呀。”

===Original===
One night, a year after she had taken her last lesson from the Wizard, she arose and returned to his cave, and he raised his eyes and saw her standing before him as formerly.

“What do you want?” he said. “I have taught you all I know.”

“You have taught me much,” she said, falling on her knees beside him, “yet I am ignorant of one thing—teach me that also—*how to be happy*.”

===Chinese===
上完巫师的最后一课，又过了一年。一天夜里，她起身回到他的洞里；老人抬起眼睛，看见她还像从前一样站在自己面前。

“你还来做什么？”他说，“我所知道的，都已经教给你了。”

“你教了我许许多多，”她说着，在他身旁跪了下来，“可有一件事我还不懂——把这个也教给我吧——*怎样才能够幸福*。”

===Original===
“Nay,” said the Wizard with a very mournful smile; “I cannot teach you that, for I do not know it myself. Go and ask it of them who know and are wiser than I.”

Then the Princess left the cave and wandered down to the seashore. All that night she spent sitting on a rock that jutted out into the sea, watching the wild sky and the moon coming and going behind the clouds. The sea dashed up around her, and the wind blew, but she did not fear them, and when the sun rose the waters were still and the wind fell. A skylark rose from the fields and flew straight up to heaven, singing as though his heart would burst with pure joy.

===Chinese===
“不，”巫师说着，脸上挂一个十分凄凉的微笑，“这个我教不了你，因为我自己也不知道。去找那些懂得它、比我更聪明的人问吧。”

于是公主离开洞穴，一路徘徊到海边。那一整夜，她都坐在一块探进海里的礁石上，望着狂野的天空，望着月亮在云后出没。海浪在她四周飞溅，风呼呼地吹，她却什么都不怕；待太阳升起，水面上风平浪静，风也息了。一只云雀从田野里飞起，直上青天，唱得仿佛那颗小小的心要因纯粹的欢乐而迸裂似的。

===Original===
“Surely that bird is happy,” said the Princess to herself; and she called it in its own tongue.

“Why do you sing?” she asked.

“I sing because I am so happy,” answered the lark.

===Chinese===
“这只鸟儿准是幸福的。”公主自言自语道。于是她用云雀自己的话唤住了它。

“你为什么唱歌？”她问。

“我唱，是因为我太快活了。”云雀答道。

===Original===
“And why are you so happy?” asked the Princess.

“So happy?” said the lark. “God is so good. The sky is so blue, and the fields are so green. Is that not enough to make me happy?”

“Teach me, then, that I may be happy too,” said Princess Fernanda.

===Chinese===
“那你为什么这么快活？”公主问。

“这么快活？”云雀说，“上帝这么仁慈，天这么蓝，田野这么绿——这还不足以让我快活吗？”

“那么教教我吧，好叫我也这样幸福。”费尔南达公主说。

===Original===
“I cannot,” said the lark; “I don’t know how to teach;” and then he rose, singing, into the blue overhead, and Princess Fernanda sighed and turned back towards the palace.

Outside her door she met her little lapdog, who barked and jumped for joy on seeing her.

===Chinese===
“我教不了，”云雀说，“我不知道怎么教。”说罢它便一路唱着，飞升到头顶的碧空里去了。费尔南达公主叹了口气，转身回宫去。

门外，她遇见自己的小哈巴狗，那狗一见她，欢喜得又跳又叫。

===Original===
“Little dog,” she said; “poor little dog, are you so glad to see me? Why are you so happy?”

“Why am I so happy?” said the little dog, surprised. “I have plenty to eat, and a soft cushion to rest upon, and you to caress me. Is not it enough to make me happy?”

“It is not enough for me,” said the Princess, sighing; but the little dog only wagged his tail and licked her hand.

===Chinese===
“小狗儿，”她说，“可怜的小狗儿，见了我你这么欢喜吗？你为什么这样快活？”

“我为什么这样快活？”小狗儿吃了一惊，“我有吃不完的食，有软软的垫子睡，还有你抚爱我——这还不足以让我快活吗？”

“这对我来说可不够。”公主叹息道。可小狗儿只是摇着尾巴，舔她的手。

===Original===
Inside her room was the Princess’s favourite little maid Doris, folding up her dresses.

“Doris,” she said, “you look very merry. Why are you so happy?”

“Please your Royal Highness, I am going to the fair,” answered Doris, “and Luke is to meet me there; only,” she added, pouting a little, “I wish I had a pretty new hat to wear with my new dress.”

===Chinese===
她的房间里，公主最宠爱的小侍女多丽丝（Doris）正在替她叠衣裳。

“多丽丝，”她说，“瞧你一脸欢喜。你为什么这样快活？”

“回殿下的话，我要去赶集，”多丽丝答道，“卢克（Luke）要在那儿跟我碰头。只是——”她微微撅起嘴，又添了一句，“我真希望有顶漂亮的新帽子，配我的新衣裳。”

===Original===
“Then you are not perfectly happy, so you cannot teach me,” said Princess Fernanda, and then she sighed again.

In the evening at sunset she arose, and went out into the village, and at the door of the first cottage to which she came, sat a woman nursing a baby, and hushing it to sleep. The baby was fat and rosy, and the mother looked down at it proudly.

===Chinese===
“那么你算不得十全十美的快活，也就教不了我了。”费尔南达公主说着，又叹了口气。

傍晚日落时分，她起身走到村子里去。她来到的第一家农舍门前，坐着一个女人，正给怀里的婴儿喂奶，哄他睡觉。婴儿白白胖胖、红红润润，做母亲的低头望着他，一脸骄傲。

===Original===
The Princess stopped, and spoke to her.

“You have a fine little child there,” she said. “Surely you must be very happy.”

The woman smiled.

===Chinese===
公主停下脚步，同她搭话。

“你这小宝宝真好，”她说，“你一定非常幸福吧。”

那女人微微一笑。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said, “so I am; only just now my goodman is out fishing, and as he’s rather late, it makes me anxious.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”她说，“我确实幸福。只是我家那口子这会儿出海打鱼去了，回来得晚了些，叫人心里惦记。”

===Original===
“Then you could not teach me,” said the Princess, sighing to herself as she moved away. She wandered on till she came to a church, which she entered. All was still within, for the church was empty; but before the altar, on a splendid bier, lay the body of a young man, who had been killed in the war. He was dressed in his gay uniform, and his breast was covered with medals, and his sword lay beside him. He was shot through the heart, but his face was peaceful and his lips were smiling. The Princess walked to his side, and looked at the quiet face. Then she stooped and kissed the cold forehead, and envied the soldier. “If he could speak,” she said, “he surely could teach me. No living mouth could ever smile like that.” Then she looked up and saw a white angel standing on the other side of the bier, and she knew it was Death.

===Chinese===
“那你教不了我了。”公主暗自叹息着走开了。她又一路走去，来到一座教堂，便走了进去。堂内一片寂静，因为教堂里空无一人；只有祭坛前一座华丽的灵床上，躺着一位青年的遗体，他是在战争中阵亡的。他穿着一身鲜艳的军装，胸前挂满了勋章，佩剑放在身旁。他被子弹射穿了心脏，面容却安详，唇边还带着微笑。公主走到他身旁，端详着那张宁静的脸，随后俯下身去，吻了吻那冰冷的额头，心里羡慕着这士兵。“他若还能说话，”她说，“一定能教给我；活人的嘴，谁也笑不出那样的笑。”这时她抬起头来，只见灵床另一侧立着一位白衣天使，她知道，那便是死神（Death）。

===Original===
“You have taught him,” she said, holding out her arms. “Will you not teach me to smile like that?”

“Nay,” said Death, pointing to the medals on the dead man’s breast, “I taught him whilst he was doing his duty. I cannot teach you.” And so saying he vanished from her sight.

===Chinese===
“你教过他了，”她张开双臂说，“你不愿也教教我，让我那样微笑吗？”

“不，”死神指着亡人胸前的勋章说，“我是在他尽本分的时候教会他的。你，我教不了。”说罢，他便从她眼前消失了。

===Original===
She went out from the church down to the seashore. There was a high sea, and a great wind, a little child had been playing on a row of rocks, and had slipped off them into the water, and was struggling among the waves, and would soon be drowned, for he was beyond his depth in the water.

When the Princess saw him, she plunged into the water and swam to where the child was, and taking him in her arms, placed him safely on the rocks again, but the waves were so strong that she could scarcely keep above them. As she tried to seize the rocks, she saw Death coming over the water towards her, and she turned to meet him gladly.

===Chinese===
她走出教堂，下到海边。海上浪高风急。方才有个小孩子在一排礁石上玩耍，一失脚滑进了水里，正在浪涛中挣扎，眼看就要淹死了，因为那水早已没过了他的头顶。

公主一看见，便纵身跳进水里，游到孩子那里，把他抱在怀中，稳稳放回礁石上；可浪头实在凶猛，她勉强才浮得出水面。她正想抓住礁石，忽见死神踏着波涛向她走来，便欢欢喜喜地转身迎了上去。

===Original===
“Now,” said he, clasping her in his arms, “I will teach you all you want to know;” and he drew her under the water, and she died.

===Chinese===
“这下好了，”他说着，把她拥进怀里，“你想知道的一切，我都教你。”说着他把她曳入水中，她便死去了。

---

===Original===
The Kings servants found her lying on the shore, with her face white and her lips cold, but smiling as they had never smiled before, and her face was very calm. They carried her home, and she was laid out in great state, covered with gold and silver.

“She was so wise,” sobbed her little maid, as she placed flowers in the cold hand, “she knew everything.”

===Chinese===
国王的仆从们发现她躺在岸上，脸色苍白，嘴唇冰凉，唇边却带着前所未有的微笑，面容安然极了。他们把她抬回宫去，以极隆重的仪节装殓，周身覆着金银。

“她那么聪明，”她的小侍女抽泣着，往那冰冷的手里放上一朵朵花，“她什么都懂啊。”

===Original===
“Not everything,” said the skylark from the window; “for she asked me, ignorant though I am, to teach her how to be happy.”

“That was the one thing I could not teach her,” said the old Wizard, looking at the dead Princess’s face. “Yet I think now she must be wiser than I, and have learned that too. For see how she smiles.”

===Chinese===
“并不是什么都懂，”窗口的云雀说道，“因为她还请教过什么都不知道的我，求我教她怎样得到幸福呢。”

“那正是我唯一教不了她的一样东西，”老巫师望着亡公主的脸说，“不过我想，她如今必定比我更聪明了，连那一样也学会了。你瞧，她笑得多好啊。”
